Rumor had it that Vice Admiral Iceberg was a woman who was very strict when it came to knowledge. She couldn't stand the sight of a bunch of illiterates under her command, so every day aboard the Golden Dream, there were general-knowledge and reading classes, and every day she forced the crew to take turns attending them.

Wishing he could forget it, Danitz said, "It's much harder than fighting!

"Because of this, we're usually unable to recruit enough crew members. Every time we dock at a port to resupply, people resign..."

He didn't continue the topic. Looking at the dealer, he said as though to himself, "Help me keep an eye out for Steel's whereabouts."

"Steel Maveti? Admiral of Blood's second mate?" Alger looked down at Blazing's left arm, which was slightly propped up by a splint, and asked, "Were you attacked?

"For the key?"

"His brain has already been eaten by his zombies!" Danitz emphasized.

"You want revenge on him?" Alger guessed from the other man's tone and request.

"Heh heh." Danitz smiled without replying, appearing focused on his new hole card.

Alger thought for a moment before saying, "The last time your captain appeared was seven days ago, near Sonia Island. That was confirmed by telegram. The Golden Dream couldn't reach Bayam so quickly.

"Do you have a new helper? If it's just you, you aren't a match for Steel even if he's alone. And as you know, he's always attended by a crowd."

Danitz's response to Alger's question was to toss out a chip.

"Call!"

"Who is it?" Alger first took Danitz's attitude as tacit confirmation. Since asking carried no risk, he pinned his hopes on the question.

Danitz stared at the exposed cards and answered simply, "You wouldn't know him."

I wouldn't know him? Someone strong enough to deal with Steel Maveti should possess at least a little fame at sea, regardless of whether he's a pirate or an adventurer... Unless he belongs to a particular secret organization, or this is his first time traveling the seas. Of course, it's also possible that Danitz doesn't wish to answer for fear of exposing a secret. That's the most probable explanation... Someone belonging to a particular organization, traveling the seas for the first time, with the strength to deal with Steel Maveti... Alger looked up slightly as a bold conjecture suddenly occurred to him.

He tapped the edge of the table and asked as though chatting about the weather, "Was Bansy Harbor fun?"

He emphasized the word "fun."

Danitz turned his head in surprise and blurted out, "How did you know?"

He believed that, given the Church of Storms' style, it was impossible for them to air their dirty laundry. Furthermore, the passengers of the White Agate had only arrived the previous night. Everyone who had witnessed the events had signed confidentiality agreements, so how could the news have spread so quickly?

Alger smiled without answering.

At that moment, he understood the value of Emperor Roselle's words: Boldly hypothesize and carefully verify!

Danitz put away his winnings and muttered, "It was no big deal. There was a revival of certain ancient customs that led to the fall of a Storm bishop."

As expected... Alger chuckled and said, "I'll help you keep an eye out for Steel.

"But how can I contact you?"

"Hmm... 15 Amyris Avenue is an unoccupied house. Write the information on a piece of paper and throw it inside," Danitz answered hesitantly.

Alger nodded and stood, patting Blazing Danitz on the shoulder.

"Don't forget my payment."

He turned and walked toward the door.

Watching the captain of the ghost ship depart, Danitz couldn't help but grumble.

"This fellow isn't bad.

"However, I have to leave this place too."

He couldn't quite trust Alger. Perhaps, in a few minutes, he might return at the head of Steel Maveti and his pirates.

After leaving the casino, Alger, who wore a pair of local baggy pantaloons, strolled down the street. He entered a department store, found a counter, and took out a handful of bronze pennies with a smile.

"Give me a deck of tarot cards."

As he waited, he leisurely considered a question:

What is Mr. Fool's Blessed doing right now?

At Old John's Restaurant.

Klein watched as the waiter placed a plate of grilled fish before him. It was wrapped in something resembling straw and covered with all manner of spices, some of which he didn't recognize.

The rich aroma wafted into Klein's nose and made his mouth water.

As expected of the Spice Archipelago... Klein was about to pick up his knife and fork when he saw the waiter place two objects resembling twigs on his plate.

Chopsticks? Klein was stunned.

Then he quickly identified the suspect: Roselle Gustav!

"This is the kind of cutlery you have to use when eating grilled stickleback. It's said that Emperor Roselle drew inspiration from elven customs," the waiter explained.

Elven customs? They are indeed a race fond of cooking and eating delicacies... Or rather, that's merely an excuse Roselle came up with... Klein guessed, largely based on his understanding of that particular person's character.

That morning, he had visited several church hospitals in Bayam to provide hospice care to dying patients, help them fulfill their last wishes, and engage in a deeper level of acting, but he had failed to find a suitable target.

It wasn't that no one died in the hospitals. Rather, they all had relatives who either accompanied them or had witnessed their deaths. Apart from frightening people, there was no possibility of disguising himself as the deceased.

I'll head to a bar where adventurers gather. There should be plenty of foreigners who, in pursuit of wealth at sea, might die in some dark corner like stray dogs while their families never hear from them again... Klein suppressed his thoughts and focused on the delicious food.

Chapter 522: Colony

The quality of the stickleback's meat certainly wasn't as good as murloc meat, but the spices worked together to form distinct layers of flavor that delighted Klein. After the first bite, he simply couldn't stop eating.

Actually, there are local Beyonders who wish to leave this dangerous circle and lead normal lives. They could easily go to Backlund and open a Rorsted restaurant, with grilled fish as their specialty. Given the city's receptiveness to different things, business definitely wouldn't be bad. The only problem is that many spices aren't as cheap there as they are here. The costs would be very high, and they'd need to choose a location suited to their target clientele... Klein put down his rather crude chopsticks and wiped his mouth with a napkin, allowing his thoughts to wander.

In his view, commoners were unable to find ways to become rich largely because their horizons weren't broad enough. Yet one's horizons were also limited by one's education and daily experiences. Constrained by social class, it was truly difficult to escape and break through those limitations. The most effective solution was to strive for a higher level of education, while the second was to take risks and venture forth. Of course, the risks were enormous, and many people vanished without a sound while walking that path.

Klein spent 2 soli and 5 pence on the meal. It wasn't cheap, but he had always been willing to spend money on good food. Besides, Danitz had recently been covering most of his expenses.

Tugging at his collar, putting on his hat, and carrying his black cane, he walked out of Old John's Restaurant just in time to see a police officer driving a tramp off the street.

The natives of the Rorsted Archipelago had darker skin than the people of the Southern Continent. It was close to the bronze color often produced by exposure to the sun. Their hair was mostly dark and naturally curled ever so slightly. They looked quite different from the colonists of the Loen Kingdom.

It had been less than fifty years since the place was fully colonized. At first, Loen had worked with the local kings and chieftains under the banner of the Central Sonia Company to extract economic benefits. Later, however, the company's management quickly descended into corruption as its members fought for power, even provoking enemies and starting wars for personal gain. More absurdly still, they reported one another, accusing their rivals of accepting bribes. For such matters, each would find a Member of Parliament to back him. During parliamentary hearings, they attacked one another so fiercely that it nearly resulted in lawsuits.[1]

The natives could never have imagined that the powerful figures who made their kings and chieftains bow, kiss the soles of their shoes, and deliver cartloads of gifts were actually insignificant people who weren't even Members of Parliament in Backlund. Although most came from noble families, they stood at the very end of the line of succession.

After that dispute, the King and Prime Minister agreed to buy out the shares, dissolve the Central Sonia Company, and dispatch the fleet and army to take full control of the Rorsted Archipelago, bringing it under true colonial rule.

At present, the archipelago was governed by the governor-general's office, Parliament, and the Courts. The upper echelons were all Loenese, while some middle-ranking positions--Members of Parliament and court magistrates--were held by descendants of the original kings and chieftains. As for lower-ranking posts, they were open to educated natives of the region. This included police officers below the rank of superintendent.

The policeman driving off the tramp with his baton was a native, and his target was likewise plainly of Rorsted descent.

As soon as the policeman saw Klein in his double-breasted frock coat, half top hat, and black gentleman's cane, he immediately put away his baton, straightened, brought his feet together, and saluted.

"Good afternoon, sir.

"How may I help you?"

Klein experienced mixed emotions as he gently nodded.

"Are there no carriages here?"

"According to the regulations of the governor-general's office, carriages aren't allowed on this street. You'll have to walk to the street ahead," the policeman explained with both fear and enthusiasm.

"Thank you." Klein casually praised him. "You speak good Loenese."

The policeman was so pleasantly surprised that he grew excited.

"I think--I think this is an essential quality for a good policeman to possess."

He had originally wanted to say that he considered himself Loenese too, but he feared that the gentleman before him would become angry.

Klein sighed inwardly and slowly walked toward the corner of the street.

Along the way, he saw that the local style of dress differed greatly from that in mainland cities such as Backlund and Tingen. It even differed from ports such as Damir and Bansy, which had been colonized for more than two hundred years.

A respectable man from Loen would wear a formal suit, a top hat, and a tie while carrying a gentleman's cane. This made the people around him submissive, afraid to meet his eyes or brush against him. The other natives and people of mixed blood favored thick jackets paired with baggy trousers and caps from the mainland. They disliked black, preferring brown, tawny, and light gray. Klein found it rather strange, but it also gave him the feeling of having arrived in a foreign country.

Of course, natives and mixed-bloods of higher status also imitated the Loenese style of dress, believing it to be a sign of civilization.

At two in the afternoon, at the Swordfish Bar, a recognized gathering place for adventurers.

There weren't many customers, so Klein easily wove between the tables and reached the bar.

He discovered one difference from other establishments: three blackboards stood beside the bar on wooden supports. Yellowing notices had been written on them in white chalk, their contents varied and strange. Some sought bodyguards, some requested help finding people, some asked for investigations into the situation on a particular island, and some offered high rewards for the heads of particular pirates, while others claimed to possess a treasure map and wished to form a team. In short, the work divided among private detectives and security companies in the Loen Kingdom still belonged to adventurers here.

"A glass of Zarhar." Klein tapped the bar counter.

It was a local malt beer, cheap and delicious, with a distinctive flavor. Adventurers loved it, something Klein had learned from Blazing Danitz.

"Three pence." The bartender casually glanced at the customer, his attitude unchanged by the stranger's unfamiliar face.

Beer in hand, Klein sat on a high stool before the bar, sipping little by little as he quietly listened to the drinkers around him. Through their conversations, he searched for a suitable target.

After nearly an hour, as the number of people in the bar increased, Klein finally heard something potentially useful.

His spirits lifted, and he listened with greater concentration.

Four people sat at a table less than three meters away, lamenting the fate of a man named Wendt.

"I always thought Wendt was out at sea. I didn't expect him to be at home. He was terribly ill."

"Sigh, if I'd knocked on his door two days earlier, he might not have died. You have no idea how terrifying the room was. Mushrooms grew in great white swaths all over his body."

"Dogshit! Stop it! Can't you see I'm eating sausages?"

"Yes, yes. Wendt's room was filled with bugs, moths, flies, butterflies, bees, and cockroaches. Holy Lord of Storms, I couldn't believe a human could live in such a place. Even the police who came later were stunned!"

As the conversation reached his ears, Klein frowned slightly, sensing that Wendt's death wasn't normal. Within only a few days of his death, his corpse was already covered in mushrooms, and insects crawled all over the room.

Something Beyonder-related? With such an anomaly, the police would definitely report it to the Mandated Punisher team... It sounds as though it happened three or four days ago. Whatever needed to be handled should already have been dealt with... Klein seriously considered whether he should pay the place a visit. At the very least, the man named Wendt had been a lone adventurer in Bayam. None of his companions wished to help him convey news of his death.

After listening for a long time, he gained a rough idea of where Wendt had rented a room. It was nearby, at 47 Blackhorn Street.

Having drained the last drop of Zarhar beer, Klein put on his hat, left the bar, and headed for the apartment.

After entering the building, he half-closed his eyes and murmured to himself, "The room where someone recently died."

He repeated the statement seven times, then used his cane to find his way swiftly and easily to the room Wendt had once occupied.

It hadn't yet been rented out, and the anomaly inside had already been dealt with. Nothing seemed amiss.

Klein put away the slip of paper he had used to open the door, locked the door behind him, and carefully walked around.

After confirming the situation, he took out extracts, essential oils, herbs, powders, and special candles, quickly setting up a spirit-channeling ritual before the bed.

Although several days had passed, making it possible to obtain only the most superficial, fragmented remnants of information, Klein believed that something was better than nothing.

Naturally, he prayed to himself and entered the space above the gray fog. He responded to the prayer and bestowed upon himself the power to channel spirits.

The candle flame suddenly soared, tinged with a ghostly blue as it swayed.

Klein felt everything fall silent, as though he had entered a realm that didn't belong to reality.

His pupils became completely black, even expelling the whites of his eyes.

He no longer needed to employ the technique of dream divination. Having advanced to Faceless, and with the aid of the gray fog's initial incursion into the real world, he could directly see Wendt's lingering spirituality, a will that refused to dissipate.

There were three scenes. In the first, the tall, thin, dark, curly-haired, sharp-featured Wendt approached an abandoned corpse and stared in astonishment as a glimmer emerged from it, condensing into a green gem suffused with an aura of vitality. In the second, Wendt lay on his bed with his eyes closed and his mouth slightly open. His skin was covered in mushrooms of every kind, while cockroaches and moths piled atop one another around him. On his chest hung a silver necklace set with the same green gem. In the third, a pretty girl with flaxen hair sat by the sea, her eyes slightly moist. Wendt's reluctant voice lingered around her.

"Raine, I'm about to die. I deeply regret never telling you that I love you. I want you to marry me..."

The scene shattered, and the spirit channeling ended. Klein looked around and saw that the room remained dark and gloomy.

This fellow was truly unlucky... Klein shook his head and sighed.

He already had a rough idea of the cause of Wendt's death--his chance discovery of something he shouldn't have picked up.

The vast majority of Beyonders didn't know about the Law of Beyonder Characteristics Indestructibility and Conservation, nor had they ever imagined that deceased Beyonders could release characteristics that became ingredients. As the process was relatively slow, these characteristics were easily overlooked. Therefore, after killing a Beyonder, people usually searched the corpse and discarded it, allowing passersby like Wendt to chance upon the remains, or perhaps creatures at the bottom of the sea or in the wilderness.

Wendt hadn't known it was a Beyonder characteristic and had mistaken it for a magical gem. Thus, he fashioned it into a necklace and kept it close to his body. Slowly corrupted by its influence, he died in agony.

[1] Author's Note: This is what the British East India Company did in India. As expected of a bureaucratic empire, it has everything you need in this regard. I nearly laughed myself to death while doing the research.

Chapter 523: Cooperation

By the time he left 47 Blackhorn Street, Klein had learned the broad outline of Wendt's circumstances.

He had disguised himself as a police officer and questioned Wendt's neighbors.

Symeem Island, the outermost island of the Rorsted Archipelago. From Bayam, it takes four or five hours to reach by liner, and there are only two departures each day, at nine and ten in the morning. Wendt's parents died long ago, and he has no relatives. The only person involved is the girl he could never forget, Raine. She's the ideal target for my first performance of acting as someone else. But just thinking about having to confess on Wendt's behalf feels terribly awkward... If the girl agrees... Damn, how am I supposed to bring matters to a close... Klein thought with a troubled expression.

He tried to recall novels he had read and films and television dramas he had watched, hoping to find the perfect solution.

Soon, he had a general idea of what to do, and his mood stabilized in the process. His thoughts began drifting toward Steel Maveti.

I hope Miss Magician obtains a radio transceiver as soon as possible... Klein sighed inwardly and boarded a rental carriage.

Backlund, Cherwood Borough.

Fors opened a letter Xio had brought back.

It came from the science-fiction novelist Aville, who was pleased to share with Ma'am Wall his thoughts on the future development and applications of radio technology.

Fors skipped directly over the beginning and middle, her eyes sweeping toward the end.

He introduced three types, complete with detailed addresses and approximate prices. The most expensive is only twelve pounds. Fors nodded slightly, feeling that this wasn't a particularly important transaction.

She suddenly felt that her sense of wealth had become somewhat inflated. Perhaps because she had witnessed so many transactions involving hundreds or thousands of pounds at the Tarot Club, she no longer paid much attention to anything in the range of ten pounds.

Mr. World has both the resources and the money. In the future, I might need to buy items from him or sell him things I don't need. Yes, I'll simply add the postage, my transportation expenses, and the cost of the ritual materials to the original price... Fors quickly made up her mind and subconsciously looked out the window.

Backlund remained gloomy and dark beneath a pattering drizzle. However, the fog wasn't as thick as before.

I wonder when I'll receive the Spirit Eater's stomach pouch from The Sun... Fors could hardly wait to advance.

In the Berg household in the City of Silver.

Having already prepared the Spirit Eater's stomach pouch and the materials Mr. Hanged Man wished to purchase, Derrick was in no hurry to sacrifice them to Mr. Fool.

He planned to wait a little longer, until the Chief was leading an expedition team or presiding over a particular sacrificial ritual, before making the attempt.

This is the safest and most reliable way... Yes, be careful. Be cautious! Derrick reminded himself before opening the Giant King's Court--Book of Blackrock, Hand-Copied Edition.

Recently, he had been reading this ancient tome. From it, he had learned something of the Giant King's Court as it had appeared in ancient times.

According to the ancient tome, it was a divine kingdom!

Time seemed frozen there, as though suspended in an eternal dusk. Every building was extraordinarily grand and magnificent, reaching even into the clouds.

Humans walking within it were exceptionally tiny, and they revered the master of that place from the depths of their hearts.

Backlund, Hillston Borough, Waymandy's residence.

After listening to an explanation, Emlyn White combed back his hair with one hand and deliberately asked, "Lord Baron, I don't remember where I heard it, but during the Second Epoch or the first half of the Third Epoch, there was a very famous city called the City of Silver."

Waymandy was a Sanguine baron more than two hundred years old. He didn't look elderly; instead, he resembled a mature gentleman in his early thirties.

His black hair was neatly combed back, and he wore a dark red cotton shirt while holding a brown pipe. Enjoying the warmth of the fireplace, he said thoughtfully, "No. At least in my memory, there was no City of Silver before the Cataclysm."

Before Emlyn could revel in his delight, Waymandy continued to himself, "But there was a Kingdom of Silver. It was originally ruled by the Giant King's Court and later brought directly under its jurisdiction."

Kingdom of Silver? Emlyn White thought for a while and said, "Lord Baron, do you know anything more specific?"

Waymandy looked up at him, smiled, and said with a hint of reminiscence, "The Kingdom of Silver held a rather special position in the Giant King's Court. Its people didn't directly believe in the Giant King Aurmir. Instead, they worshiped the queen of the Giant King's Court, Omebella."

City of Generosity, Bayam, dock district. Acid Lemon Street, Wind of Azure Inn.

Klein stood at the corner of the street, removed the topaz pendant from his left wrist, and performed a divination to ensure there was no danger ahead.

With that settled, he strolled back to the inn, went up to the third floor, and opened the door of the luxurious suite.

He was slightly surprised to find Blazing Danitz already back, slumped in his reclining chair and drinking.

After a moment's consideration, Klein calmly asked, "What time is it?"

"Isn't there a clock?" Danitz muttered.

He looked at the opposite wall and read out the time.

"Three forty in the afternoon..."

Before he finished, Danitz suddenly came to his senses. Sitting upright, he laughed dryly.

"I've already visited every place where I can obtain information and asked everyone I can ask. There's no need to stay outside any longer. It would only increase the risk of exposure and affect your hunting plan!"

Klein found a chair and sat down. Expressionless, he said, "Tell me what happened."

"What problems could there be? Although I'm primarily an adventurer who hunts for treasure, I'm also a qualified part-time pirate." Danitz felt that his abilities had been insulted.

After Gehrman Sparrow swept him with a cold glance, he smiled and briefly recounted the places he had visited, the people he had met, and the news he had heard that morning and afternoon.

Hearing Danitz lament that a ghost ship captain named Alger knew about Bansy Harbor, Klein suddenly felt like frowning.

Given Mr. Hanged Man's connection to the Church of Storms, he didn't know about the anomaly at Bansy Harbor before I controlled The World and deliberately mentioned it, so how could this Alger have known? Is he a high-ranking powerhouse of the Church of Storms disguised as a pirate, or someone connected to the heretics at Bansy Harbor? Hmm... Mr. Hanged Man has been active in the surrounding seas, so it's possible that this is him... Klein's heart stirred as he recalled The Hanged Man's characteristics.

Above the gray fog, although he couldn't clearly see the other members' appearances, it was still obvious whether they were male or female and what color their hair was!

Klein raised a hand to stop Danitz and said in a low voice, "Very messy hair, dark blue in color?"

"You know him? That fellow isn't simple!" Danitz exclaimed.

As expected... Heh heh, I truly didn't expect to encounter him so easily... Klein didn't respond. Instead, he leaned forward and said, "Continue."

Without thinking too much about it, Danitz gave a general account of what happened afterward and explained himself.

"As you know, no one can be certain when they'll obtain results while gathering information. After finding everyone there is to find, all one can do is wait patiently. This will definitely take some time."

"There's another solution that doesn't require waiting," Klein deliberately said in a flat tone.

"What solution?" Danitz asked in surprise.

Klein nudged his gold-rimmed glasses, the corners of his mouth curving into a smile.

"Use bait."

Bait? Danitz stared at him, momentarily puzzled.

In only a second, realization dawned.

He could be the only bait!

When fishing for an enemy, no one cared whether the bait was swallowed. The only concern was whether the prey could be drawn out!

Simply put, "bait" was a highly dangerous occupation!

"Haha, that isn't a great solution. Yes, that's what my intuition tells me. I should be able to gain something at the Red Theater. I'll go there and take a look!" Danitz grabbed his coat and eagerly rushed out the door.

Klein had originally planned to follow Blazing from a distance to see whether he could find any clues, but he suddenly heard layers of illusory prayers.

They came from a man.

Klein paused thoughtfully and turned into the washroom.

Ten seconds later, he appeared above the gray fog. He saw the crimson star representing The Hanged Man continually burgeon and shrink.

As expected... Klein sat down, leaned back in his chair, and extended his spirituality.

The Hanged Man's voice became clearer:

"Honorable Mr. Fool, I am presently investigating a key connected to Vice Admiral Iceberg. I encountered Blazing Danitz at a casino and learned that he witnessed the anomaly at Bansy Harbor. I also learned that he plans to join forces with a powerful figure to deal with Steel Maveti.

"I suspect that Blazing Danitz is working with your Blessed, so I prayed to you.

"If that is indeed the case, and if he wishes to receive a certain degree of assistance, I can be of help."

Mr. Hanged Man has indeed guessed Gehrman Sparrow's identity... He probably only had his suspicions, but successfully verified them by asking Danitz about Bansy Harbor... This is also good. With the help of an experienced local, the plan to hunt Steel Maveti will proceed much more smoothly... Unless it directly concerns The Fool, there's no reason for The Hanged Man to entrap a Blessed... Judging from his expression and attitude, he doesn't yet harbor any suspicions toward The Fool... I haven't left any loopholes... Klein's thoughts raced, and he quickly reached a decision.

After performing a divination, he conjured The World and covered the dummy in fog. He placed him in a prayerful posture and had him answer solemnly, "Honorable Mr. Fool, I require some assistance."

After completing the scene, Klein cast the voice and image into the crimson star symbolizing The Hanged Man.

Chapter 524: Meeting

At five in the afternoon, at Pam's Divination Lodge on Olive Tree Avenue.

Alger Wilson pushed open a brown wooden door with glass panes set above it and entered the mysticism-themed cafe.

He ordered a cup of Fermo coffee from the Paz Valley on the Star Highlands of the Southern Continent. Then he took out the tarot cards he had bought earlier and placed them beside him. The top card was The Hanged Man, depicting an angel suspended upside down with his hands bound behind his back.

Unlike that morning, he had changed into a dark, classical robe and wore a clergyman's biretta, resembling a warlock or magician from folklore.

After drawing a silent breath, Alger slowly sipped his coffee. He showed no anxiety as he waited.

After five or six minutes, the heavy brown wooden door opened again, and a young man in a black tweed coat and half top hat entered.

The gentleman appeared not even thirty years old. His face was thin and angular, and his bearing was both mature and gloomy. It was none other than Klein, who had slightly altered his appearance and changed his persona.

He wasn't wearing gold-rimmed glasses, but his vision remained unaffected. With a casual sweep of his eyes, his gaze settled on Alger's dark blue sideburns.

Klein looked down and saw The Hanged Man lying faceup atop the deck.

Without a word, he walked over, removed his hat, and sat opposite Alger. With a grim smile, he said, "I'd like a divination."

As he spoke, he had already taken in the appearance of the Tarot Club's most senior member.

Deep-set features, a rugged outline, and an unmistakably weathered look. He's clearly adept at fighting and spends a great deal of time outdoors.

His skin is bronze, but it differs from that of the natives. He looks like a pure-blooded Loenese who has been battered by the elements for years, but the dark blue hair is more unusual, something not found among the Loenese. It's closer to the colonists from Desi Bay who live around the Berserk Sea.

Mixed blood... Klein concluded.

Alger looked at the gentleman opposite him, slowly superimposing his image over that of The World. Then he pushed the tarot cards across the table and said in a low voice, "This requires you to shuffle and cut the cards yourself."

Klein reached out, picked them up, and spread them fully to inspect them. Then he gathered them together and reshuffled.

He cut the deck repeatedly, drew three cards, and arranged them as past, present, and future.

Klein slowly leaned back, but with his right hand he turned over the middle tarot card. It depicted a naked woman wearing only a purple silk scarf, surrounded by a green wreath resembling a doorway.

This was The World, numbered 21. Number 22 returned to 0, symbolizing The Fool.

"How should I interpret this?" Klein deliberately asked.

Although The Hanged Man hadn't explicitly stated that The World was The Fool's Blessed, Klein didn't believe there was any need to pretend otherwise. He felt that openness would help establish his image--if Alger hadn't guessed, it would seem candid and confident. If Alger already knew, it would make The World appear at ease, as though everything remained within his grasp.

H-he knows I know? He already believes I guessed before raising the matter above the gray fog? Impressive... Alger's heart skipped a beat as he replied at a measured pace, "It's inverted. It means that matters will fail due to insufficient preparation."

"What preparations are needed?" Klein nodded thoughtfully and asked.

Alger retrieved all the tarot cards except The World and expertly shuffled and cut the deck.

Then he turned over the top card.

It was The Hierophant!

Alger's voice remained deep and low.

"You need counsel. You require the guidance of faith and religion to avoid straying down the wrong path."

Without waiting for Klein to speak, he turned over the second card. It was The Moon, gazing down upon the land.

"You will be confused. You will be exhausted, wandering through dreams, but this is only temporary."

Next, Alger handed over a third tarot card. It was The Sun.

"Everything shall pass, and light will shine upon the land," he intoned like a charlatan.

Klein remained silent for a few seconds before seemingly seeking confirmation. "Church, dream, sun?"

A trace of a smile spread across Alger's face. He nodded slightly and said, "That's right."

He had hidden hints about the subsequent plan within the tarot reading.

In fact, in a situation where he wasn't personally involved and hadn't even drawn attention, there was no need to be so circumspect. He could have directly explained everything in detail, but Alger felt he still needed to test a Blessed like The World. He wanted to know whether the man was intelligent rather than merely powerful.

If their intellects were on the same level, Alger believed they could cooperate more often in the future. Intelligent people didn't need to say too much to one another. Conversely, if The World failed the test, Alger would try not to involve him in his own affairs. He would only ask for help when he needed an assistant, unless Mr. Fool issued other orders.

Now, The World's response and prior performances had confirmed that he was cunning, vicious, and experienced.

Heh, I'm an expert in tarot cards... In such matters, Mr. Hanged Man, you're merely a novice... Klein secretly laughed and belittled him inwardly.

The message concealed in The Hanged Man's interpretation was very simple. The Hierophant meant that he intended to inform the Church of Storms about Blazing Danitz and Steel Maveti, then use the Mandated Punishers to divide the enemy and reap the benefits.

This was a tactic Klein often employed, so he had no difficulty understanding it.

The Moon and The Sun that followed were a reminder and a warning from The Hanged Man.

Since the Mandated Punishers were involved, a certain degree of caution was necessary. Based on Alger's experience, when dealing with similar matters, his colleagues in Bayam would definitely use a Sealed Artifact capable of forcing many people within its range into a dream. And given Maveti's characteristics, they would also have to use a particular item in the Sun domain that countered him.

I'm immune to dreams, so I'm not afraid of the Sun... Klein reached out and half-turned the inverted World card into its upright position, indicating that the plan was feasible and that he would make preparations.

Alger raised his head and drew a deep breath.

"The proprietor specializes in aromatherapy. She uses the fragrances of various essential oils, extracts, incense, and floral essences to treat corresponding emotional problems and calm restless minds. Would you like to try it?"

Using the contact point at Amyris Avenue? Klein smiled in response.

"All right."

The two looked at each other without moving. They said nothing more about aromatherapy, for both already understood everything.

Klein didn't remain any longer than necessary. He took out his pocket watch, glanced at it, and slowly rose.

Alger erased his smile, pressed a hand to his chest, and bowed slightly.

"Let us praise God. All divinatory revelations come from 'Him.'"

Oh, you even know how to express your loyalty... Klein suppressed his laughter and replied solemnly, imitating Alger, "Let us praise God."

He took two steps away, then suddenly stopped and looked back at Alger, chuckling as he put on his hat.

"Frankly, that attire doesn't suit you."

Huh? Alger couldn't follow Mr. World's train of thought.

After Klein left the themed cafe, Alger withdrew his gaze and studied himself in the mirror at the corner.

At first, he saw nothing wrong with what he wore. However, after The World's remark, the longer he looked, the more incongruous it appeared. Finally, he understood why the man had said that.

A fellow who looked rough and weather-beaten, like someone capable of summoning a hundred sailors at any moment to beat up an opponent or drawing an ax to chop them to pieces, truly shouldn't be wearing a mysterious, classical warlock's robe. It made his bearing seem rather peculiar.

Cathedral of Waves.

Having changed back into his original clothes, Alger kept a low profile as he followed the worshipers into the hall. Under the pretext of making a confession, he met Bishop Chogo through the priest in charge.

After saluting, he went straight to the point.

"I encountered Blazing Danitz, who claims that the key possessed by Vice Admiral Iceberg has nothing to do with Death's treasure and that they are even willing to sell it.

"He entrusted me with a mission to watch for Steel Maveti's whereabouts. Apparently, he was injured by this second mate of the Admiral of Blood and is desperate to escape his pursuit.

"Your Excellency, I wish to leak this information so that Steel Maveti and his subordinates succeed in cornering Blazing Danitz. I hope to use this opportunity to capture them all or execute them on the spot.

"This will effectively curb the pirates' arrogance."

Chogo revealed a look of approval.

"Very good. The way you handle matters is better than I expected."

Alger replied humbly, "This all stems from the Lord's guidance and from your teachings.

"This evening, I'll find a suitable target and leak the information. If I return to pray, it will indicate that Steel Maveti is temporarily taking no action. If I do not appear, it means that he or his subordinates may have restrained me to prevent the news from leaking. That will mean they have taken the bait."

After providing details of the location and other matters, Alger returned to the confessional and departed as usual.

At a quarter past seven in the evening, at the Amyris Leaf Bar.

Wearing baggy trousers and a headscarf wrapped around his dark blue hair, Alger stood beside a boxing ring with a glass of Lanti Proof in hand. A mocking expression on his face, he watched the two bruised contestants.

Before long, he noticed his target enter and head straight for the bar.

After a while, he sat beside the thin man and said with a chuckle, "I heard Steel has arrived in Bayam."

The man turned his head in alarm and replied with a false smile, "Why don't I know about it?"

"Is that so? Looks like Blazing fooled me!" Alger smacked the bar and took a mouthful of liquor.

"Blazing... Danitz?" The man's eyes lit up as he asked hesitantly.

"Yes, him!" Alger gritted his teeth. "I met him this morning at the Gold Coin Casino. That damned fellow claimed Steel was in Bayam. Ptooey! How dare he lie to me!"

The swarthy, thin man's eyes darted around, but he didn't interrupt.

He quietly listened to the end, stood, and chuckled.

"I forgot I have something to do. We should play cards another time."

He patted Alger on the shoulder and, in a seemingly unhurried manner, hastened out of the bar.

Holding his glass, Alger half-turned to watch the man's retreating back. His eyes were deep and gloomy, with no trace of a smile at the corners of his mouth.

Chapter 525: The Calm Squall

Looking up at the wall clock and seeing that it was past eight, Alger set down his glass mug and pushed through the drunkards into the street.

As the Rorsted Archipelago was rich in coal, Bayam resembled mainland cities such as Backlund and Pritz Harbor. Tall black lampposts lined its streets, and the glow of burning gas streamed through metal grilles, illuminating the relatively clean roads.

Alger pulled off his headscarf and slowly turned into a side alley. At its dead end, he smelled urine mingled with alcohol. Although the Amyris Leaf Bar had a washroom, it clearly wasn't sufficient for its customers during peak hours. Drunks who couldn't hold it could only find secluded places to relieve themselves.

Crimson moonlight pierced the clouds and shone into the alley. While Alger was still considering whether he should put on a more convincing performance, a firm, resonant voice came from behind him.

"Did you deliberately leak the information about Blazing to us?"

He's not stupid... Alger muttered inwardly as he slowly turned, as though preparing for a surprise attack.

Seven or eight paces away, he saw a figure leaning against the wall.

The figure stood about 1.78 meters tall and wore a boat-shaped hat. His face was thin and sharp, and his expression aggressive.

A lock of black hair hung down, half concealing his left dark green eye and softening his cold appearance.

Although there was often a considerable difference between a wanted poster and the actual person, many famous pirates didn't even bother disguising themselves while wandering through the city. As a Church insider, Alger had seen many nearly photographic portraits created through rituals and had participated in pirate conventions. He could still successfully connect the person before him to a name on the bounty notices.

He didn't reveal this and deliberately hesitated before asking, "Calm Squall?"

He was Steel Maveti's chief assistant, a Beyonder adept at controlling his emotions and thinking calmly, but he was also inhumanly cruel and had a bounty of 1,500 pounds. The man tugged at his black trench coat and revealed a hollow smile.

"Can I deny it?

"Well... probably not. Just as you can't deny that you deliberately mentioned Blazing to Oamaru. He isn't someone who enjoys using his brain, the complete opposite of me."

"I never intended to lie. I only want payment for the information you obtained from me. Between Blazing, who is alone, and Steel, who has so many helpers, anyone with a brain knows whom to choose. Of course, I hope you'll keep this secret. I don't wish to be hunted by Vice Admiral Iceberg," Alger replied frankly.

Squall nodded leisurely and said, "Tell me the details."

"Just as I said before. I happened to encounter and recognize Blazing at the Gold Coin Casino. He entrusted me to watch for Steel's whereabouts. Heh heh, it appears he wants to launch a counterattack," Alger said with a sneer. "We agreed on a point of contact. I believe this intelligence is worth at least 1,000 pounds."

"A thousand pounds? Look up at the crimson moon. Are you dreaming?" Squall scoffed. "This might be a trap. Don't you understand? Blazing may well have found helpers, which is why he dares to seek us out."

"It's not for me to judge whether it's a trap. Five hundred pounds. For anything less, I'd rather pretend none of this happened." Alger attempted to negotiate the payment.

"Three hundred pounds. You must follow me somewhere and remain there for a while. It's to prevent you from selling this information to others and ruining our plans. We'll pay you once we capture Blazing based on your intelligence, or after we channel his spirit. Don't worry. Food, liquor, and a bed will all be free. In any event, you've struck it lucky! If you cause any accidents, heh heh... I believe you know the consequences," Squall proposed in a tone that brooked no refusal.

Sure enough, just as I expected. Since my background is unknown and I don't appear too dangerous, they're far more likely to detain me temporarily than silence me... However, I've also prepared for the worst. As long as I'm not too careless, escaping won't be a problem... Alger deliberately acted conflicted as he said, "No more than two days, or my crew will sail away with my ship."

"I'll inform them if it exceeds two days." At some point, a sharp scalpel had appeared in Squall's palm, and he made it leap and spin like an acrobat.

After Alger described the contact point at 15 Amyris Avenue and the corresponding communication method in detail, Squall said nothing further. He turned and led Alger through a succession of turns until they reached an unknown street and entered a nondescript house.

"Long time no see, blue-haired captain of a ghost ship." The door was opened by an old man whose hair was more white than black. He wore local clothing and a pair of baggy pantaloons.

"Old Quinn. You truly are an intelligence officer for the Admiral of Blood..." Alger deliberately said in astonishment.

Old Quinn chuckled. "There's always some truth to rumors. What you imagine to be false may very well be real."

He didn't turn on the gas wall lamp, instead carrying a silver candle lantern as he guided Alger and Squall through the dark hall and into a spacious, windowless basement.

"You'll remain here for a while. My friends and I will watch you and provide food and liquor," Old Quinn said with a laugh. "To demonstrate our sincerity, we won't disarm you."

"All right." Alger took the initiative to walk to the low bed in the basement.

Old Quinn closed the heavy stone door and locked it behind him.

Squall didn't linger. He hurried away, repeatedly checking whether anyone was following him.

After switching to a rental carriage, he arrived at a Loenese enclave in Bayam. It was also the residential district of the upper class.

Upon entering a garden villa, Squall saw Maveti seated on a sofa in the living room, waiting for him. The others either lay, stood, or sat in a semicircle, with puppets and zombies guarding them.

Steel had thick lips and swarthy skin. His hair curled like the steel balls commonly seen in factories.

"Is the intelligence reliable?" The muscles in his arms trembled, emanating a sense of tremendous strength. Yet his whole being radiated a cold and sinister feeling, as though he weren't a living creature.

Squall nodded.

"He's a pirate captain who serves money. I've already detained him at Old Quinn's place. If there truly is a problem, he definitely won't leave there alive. I believe he understands that very well."

At that point, Squall revealed a cold smile.

"However, we must still guard against accidents. This might be a trap set by Blazing."

"Do you have a plan?" Maveti asked directly.

The first person he looked toward was a pirate lying on a couch. The man wore a brown local tunic and twirled a yellowish-brown straw hat around his fingers.

This was Maveti's deputy for the operation, the captain of the sixth ship lost in the battle against Vice Admiral Dusk, Blood Brambles Hendry. He had a bounty of 3,800 pounds.

"Squall clearly has a plan." Hendry covered his pale face with his straw hat.

Squall chuckled and said, "Didn't that adventurer John Smith send Mordor to infiltrate our ranks? Let's deliberately leak the news to him, making him believe we're still waiting for reinforcements and can only act the day after tomorrow or in three days.

"That bunch of greedy adventurers definitely won't spare Blazing. They'll fall over one another to get him. When the time comes, we can find a place to watch the show. If there's an accident, they'll bear the brunt of it. If everything proceeds smoothly, we can eliminate them as well!"

"All right." Steel Maveti's eyeballs shifted slightly, radiating intense bloodlust.

In the early hours of the morning, everything was ready.

Blood Brambles Hendry brought over a leather suitcase, removed a peacock-blue carpet, and slowly unfurled it before him. It was covered in mysterious patterns with an inhuman quality.

Maveti, Squall, two other Beyonders, as well as eight zombies and puppets, took their places on it.

Hendry was the last to step aboard. Half-closing his eyes, he uttered a single word in Elvish: "Flight!"

The peacock-blue carpet suddenly drew taut and floated upward, lifting everyone from the ground and carrying them through the air toward Amyris Avenue.

During the journey, Hendry pulled out a black handkerchief and flicked his wrist, transforming it into the night sky, which magically concealed all their traces beneath the moonlight.

After seven or eight minutes, they reached the vicinity of 20 Amyris Avenue. The targeted house stood diagonally opposite.

They didn't advance. Instead, they allowed the Flying Carpet to hover silently before the crown of a thick tree, where they lay prone and observed the situation.

Time passed second by second as Hendry steadied the mystical item. He showed no sign of exhausting his spirituality.

The night slowly receded, and a faint red glow suffused the horizon. The sun was about to rise, and the pirates led by Steel Maveti began preparing to find another suitable location for daytime surveillance.

At that moment, a figure moved nimbly across the rooftops with his back bent, arriving above 15 Amyris Avenue.

He wore a black cloak, with yellow eyebrows and dark blue eyes. His features and facial contours were relatively soft. It was none other than Blazing Danitz!

Danitz cautiously looked around for a moment, then climbed onto the raised chimney, reached inside, and dropped a note down it.

He's actually here? Steel Maveti, Blood Brambles Hendry, Squall, and the others all felt their spirits lift.

At that instant, several figures sprang from the chimneys and surroundings of Units 13, 14, and 17. Moving swiftly and powerfully, they rushed toward Unit 15 from different directions, some smashing through windows, some kicking open doors, and others charging down the chimney.

Chapter 526: Dream of Eternity

The Rorsted Archipelago was the Loen Kingdom's administrative center in the Central Sonia Sea. Similarly, it was an important diocese of the Church of Storms, with a high-ranking deacon who sat on the Council of Cardinals permanently stationed there.

Of course, even a pirate at the admiral level might not be enough to spur such a powerful figure into action, much less a mere second mate and boatswain. As a result, the task fell to Deacon Cordoba Roye, who gladly accepted the order and led two teams of Mandated Punishers to activate Sealed Artifacts 2-37 and 2-166. To ensure absolute safety, they lay in ambush some distance from 15 Amyris Avenue.

From Cordoba Roye's perspective, one team of Mandated Punishers equipped with two Sealed Artifacts would have been sufficient to deal with the group of pirates comprising Steel and Blazing Danitz. However, the latter's intention to monitor his pursuers' whereabouts worried him. Instinctively sensing a problem, he called in an additional team of Mandated Punishers.

Actually, 2-37 alone would be enough... As they waited, Cordoba Roye suddenly sighed.

In his opinion, this Sealed Artifact would be the nightmare of Steel, Blazing, and their companions.

He closed his eyes, and the corresponding information surfaced in his mind.

"Number: 37.

"Name: Dream of Eternity.

"Danger Grade: 2. Dangerous. It must be used carefully and in moderation. It may only be requested for operations requiring three or more people, a deacon, or a diocesan bishop.

"Security Classification: Bishop, Team Captain, and above.

"Sealing Method: Place in boiling water.

"Description: It resembles a heart. It is black, feels ice-cold to the touch, and is riddled with holes, often producing a flute-like sound.

"This item originated from a primitive tribe in West Balam on the Southern Continent. They were nocturnal and led by spirit warlocks.

"A Loenese army company conquered the tribe and obtained the object they venerated upon their altar. During the following week, soldiers began behaving abnormally, going mad, committing suicide, and so forth.

"Research indicates that anyone who comes into contact with this item will enter a prolonged dream. Unless awakened by someone else, they will remain forever in that illusory world. On the surface, they appear frustrated, fearful, and confused. Their mental and biological states gradually change.

"Even after being awakened, the researchers remained unable to distinguish reality from dreams for a long time, leading to a series of tragic incidents, including but not limited to confessing their love to superiors, kissing a venomous snake on the mouth, suffering from depression, stripping naked and running through the streets, and attempting to bathe in boiling water... Had they not been transferred to another city, their only possible outcomes would have been madness and suicide.

"This item can be used to cause all creatures in the target's vicinity to enter the same dream... The user may choose the targets to a certain extent and exclude companions... The maximum effective radius is fifty meters. Exceeding that range places an enormous burden on the user and inflicts severe mental damage... Afterward, the user will enter a state in which they cannot distinguish reality from dreams and must leave the city where 2-37 is located to have any chance of recovery.

"Numerous examples (see Appendix) demonstrate that even without direct contact, this item can greatly affect the surrounding population unless placed in boiling water...

"Appendix: 1. Its effects resemble those of a Nightmare from the Evernight pathway, but are more pronounced and uncontrollable.

"2. Example 1: A Keeper once forgot to add charcoal, causing the fire to go out and the temperature of the boiling water to fall. He soon displayed an abnormal reaction and was suspected of dreaming about a girl he loved. He began passionately confessing to his glove and attempted to engage in a relationship with it that went beyond friendship;

"Example 2..."

As his thoughts churned, Cordoba Roye suddenly saw Blazing Danitz, the Golden Dream's fourth boatswain, arrive at the contact point to collect information.

He gestured, signaling the Mandated Punishers to open the golden box engraved with numerous symbols and magic labels, then remove Sealed Artifact 2-37 from the boiling water in which it had been soaking.

The golden box itself was another Sealed Artifact: 2-166.

It could maintain the original temperature of objects within it and, to a certain extent, alter the weather to produce a rain of Sun Holy Water.

Of course, it also had extremely negative effects. Otherwise, the Mandated Punishers would have long since used it to seal 2-37 instead of treating it as a temporary measure. Its most unacceptable negative effect was that any object left in prolonged contact with it would acquire the characteristics of life and become a devout believer in the Eternal Blazing Sun. Among the Mandated Punishers, there had already appeared a table that sang praises to the Eternal Blazing Sun every day and a candle that wished to ignite itself every second to purify everything.

In response, the irascible Mandated Punishers had chopped the table into firewood and lit it with that very candle.

Seeing figures appear one after another and charge toward 15 Amyris Avenue, Cordoba Roye didn't hesitate to press down with his right hand, ordering the team member beside him to activate Sealed Artifact 2-37.

Illusory fish scales immediately appeared on the surface of the member's palm, and he reached directly into the boiling water to grasp the Sealed Artifact resembling a pitch-black heart.

The melodious sound of a flute rang out, and dense darkness enveloped the house at 15 Amyris Avenue.

The scene before the adventurer John Smith remained unchanged. He still saw the bare, unfurnished living room and the black-cloaked Blazing Danitz. Nothing made him feel as though he were dreaming.

They rushed forward with all their strength, fired their weapons, and used their powers, successfully wounding Blazing and capturing the famous pirate with a 3,000-pound bounty who was involved with Death's Key.

In the real world, however, some sat with their legs raised, some swam with their arms outstretched, and some used their fingers as guns while making shooting noises with their mouths.

"Move out!" Cordoba Roye left his hiding place and charged toward 15 Amyris Avenue. The other members of the Mandated Punisher teams divided into two groups and began surrounding the house from different directions.

Before Cordoba arrived, the living room was already brilliantly illuminated, as though the sun were rising. Pure, holy water dripped down like rain. It was none other than Sealed Artifact 2-166 in use.

Some distance away, the Flying Carpet hovered silently in the darkness. The moment Steel Maveti and the others saw the Mandated Punishers appear, they understood that this was indeed a trap set by Blazing Danitz.

"That son of a bitch actually defected to the Church of Storms!" Maveti growled, mania flashing in his eyes.

Blood Brambles Hendry looked at Calm Squall and praised him sincerely. "Fortunately, we weren't careless."

"In truth, I didn't expect a foolish donkey like Blazing Danitz to cooperate with the Church of Storms," Squall said calmly. "Perhaps, after being injured, he was captured by the Mandated Punishers and had no choice but to cooperate."

Just as they were about to leave, they saw something protrude from the shadows outside 15 Amyris Avenue. Hugging the ground, it rapidly moved away.

"Blazing!" Steel Maveti recalled the scene of his failed ambush.

"Danitz!" Hendry also recognized the man who had attempted to escape using his magical powers.

Squall was taken aback for a moment before realization dawned.

"Either Blazing Danitz wishes to seize this opportunity to escape the Mandated Punishers' control, or he had another trap prepared for us. However, for some reason, the Mandated Punishers were drawn here, leaving him no choice but to abandon it and flee."

"Regardless, this is an opportunity!" Blood Brambles Hendry glanced at the Mandated Punishers storming the house. "As long as we seize this minute, we can kill Blazing and take him away from right beneath the Mandated Punishers' noses!"

Steel Maveti's hopes instantly rose, and an abnormal mania appeared in his eyes.

After Squall nodded, he said to the other two Beyonders, "Take the zombies and puppets and guard this position. Once the Mandated Punishers arrive, use them to create chaos and seize the opportunity to escape!"

"Yes, Boss." The two Beyonder pirates jumped from the Flying Carpet together with the zombies and puppets.

The peacock-blue carpet wheeled around and shot ahead of Blazing.

"Support us. Focus on guarding the surroundings. I'm worried Danitz has other helpers," Squall promptly told Blood Brambles Hendry.

A smile appeared on Hendry's pale face.

"All right!"

Without another word, Squall nodded to Steel Maveti.

Taking advantage of his high vantage point, Maveti could distinguish the mostly normal shadows and determine the target's direction.

He abruptly leaped from the Flying Carpet and struck the ground heavily like a massive boulder. White frost condensed beneath his feet as a layer of cold, transparent ice rapidly spread outward.

The black shadow was immediately frozen in a corner!

With a crack, Blazing Danitz struggled free and "grew" out of the shadows.

On the Flying Carpet, Hendry demonstrated their excellent teamwork by dropping what he had long held in his hand: green leaves, flowers, and brambles.

The leaves, petals, and brambles grew wildly or joined together, ensnaring Blazing Danitz and rendering him immobile.

Squall seized the opportunity to leap from the Flying Carpet and thrust the sharp scalpel in his right hand toward Danitz's neck.

Splat!

Holding the scalpel's handle in his right hand, he circled behind the target.

Danitz's head fell, and his body rapidly shrank into a thin, tattered paper figurine.

At the same time, Steel Maveti felt an indescribable hunger descend upon him. It was as though a terrifying monster hidden in the darkness were silently watching him.

No, it isn't Blazing! Just as the thought flashed through his mind, an extremely compressed incandescent fireball flew from a nearby rooftop and hurtled toward him.

Steel Maveti made only one movement: he raised an arm before his body.

Boom!

Flames exploded, and a shock wave radiated in every direction, blocking Squall's and Hendry's lines of sight. Yet neither was flustered, for they had absolute confidence in Steel.

Sure enough, Maveti had only suffered torn clothes and white marks across his skin. Apart from that, he was entirely uninjured.

But at that moment, he suddenly saw a figure step out of the roiling flames in the air. The figure clearly wore Blazing Danitz's black cloak, but he had neatly combed black hair, dark and emotionless eyes, and a thin, cold face.

The previous Danitz had always been Faceless Klein--Gehrman Sparrow, who had been prepared for the hunt all along!

Chapter 527: A Quick Battle

As he leaped from the flames, two points of light in the eyes of Klein, who wore a golden glove on his left hand, rapidly enlarged and changed from dark to brilliant, like lightning suddenly splitting the sky.

After distracting Steel Maveti and his companions with Paper Figurine Substitutes, he had activated Creeping Hunger and switched to the Interrogator's soul. He planned to use the Beyonder power Psychic Piercing.

"Ah!"

Steel Maveti suddenly let out a miserable scream. It was as though someone had driven a knife into his head and stirred his grayish-white brain. Pain erupted from the depths of his soul, instantly overwhelming his mind.

At the same time, the shock caused his Beyonder powers to erupt beyond his control, transforming into a hurricane that spread in every direction.

Beautiful snowflakes suddenly began falling from the sky. Layers of translucent ice spread outward, and the trees on both sides of the road acquired white borders. They rapidly withered, revealing signs of decay.

The lingering resentment of remnant spirits was awakened in succession. They sobbed, wailed, and shrieked, dyeing the nearby area a gray and oppressive hue.

Klein descended naturally from the sky, his left glove shining like the sun.

He opened his arms and leaned back as though embracing something.

The instant his heels touched the frigid ground, a pure, blazing, dense pillar of light descended from above.

The holy radiance enveloped Steel Maveti, illuminating his dark skin, firm muscles, and curly, steel-ball-like hair.

Priest of Light, Light of Holiness!

Without a sound, Maveti's expression became even more distorted, and his skin began to melt.

At that moment, he resembled a candle tossed into a blazing fireplace.

Compared to the corrupted Bishop Millet, he was a Zombie and therefore more vulnerable to such attacks. The damage he suffered was even more severe!

The snowflakes stopped falling, and the ice on the ground rapidly melted. The trees with white borders revealed their brown color.

There was no doubt that the powerful beam of light drew the attention of Blood Brambles Hendry and Squall. The former's expression changed as he reached into a hidden pocket and withdrew spellcasting materials. He intended to use his advantage in elevation to rescue Steel Maveti.

At that moment, a raven formed entirely of flame suddenly appeared before his eyes.

One, two, three... Countless Fire Ravens filled Hendry's vision like crimson stars. They flew in from every direction, wings beating in a dense, scorching formation.

Hendry's heart skipped a beat, and his gaze seemed to freeze. He immediately moved his hand elsewhere to grasp a different material.

Whoosh!

He scattered pale blue powder over his body while chanting in ancient Hermes, "Sea Waves!"

The powder abruptly expanded, transforming into dark blue waves that spread in all directions.

When they met the Fire Ravens, they produced sizzling sounds and great quantities of white mist.

The flames from the previous explosion had subsided slightly. Through the remnants of crimson light, Squall saw Steel Maveti's skin melting like wax and his flesh dissolving inch by inch. He also saw the unfamiliar man in a black cloak standing beside him.

He didn't panic. Like a cold-blooded viper devoid of emotion, he calmly raised his arms and abruptly pressed both palms downward.

In an instant, he saw the thin, grim-faced young man's body sink. His movements slowed, as though invisible wings had been broken. Meanwhile, Steel Maveti began struggling and rolling to avoid a second Light of Holiness.

Immediately afterward, Squall noticed that the man had lost his weight and substance. Once again, he turned into a thin paper figurine, pressed against the ground by an invisible force before becoming soaked in the mud left by the melting ice.

Klein materialized in a different position. Rubbing his right thumb and middle finger together, he snapped three times as he rushed forward.

Pa! Pa! Pa!

The dark golden Sunbird-shaped brooch on his chest flashed repeatedly, adding a purifying effect to his Air Bullets.

Bang! Bang! Bang! The three bullets struck in rapid succession, hitting the slow-moving Steel Maveti in the right side of his chest. They pierced his half-melted skin and flesh, creating an exaggerated, gruesome wound from which golden flames spread in every direction.

Swoosh! Swoosh! Swoosh! Squall hurled scalpel after scalpel, but Klein evaded them all with deft, acrobatic maneuvers.

By then, Steel Maveti had caught his breath. Pushing off with his palm, he rose again and charged straight at the enemy, fully displaying his advantage in close combat and his abnormally powerful body, which could withstand tremendous blows.

Ice formed beneath his feet once more, and his fists exuded an aura of decay.

Then, once again, he saw two bolts of lightning shoot from Klein's dark brown eyes and plunge into his own.

Steel Maveti instinctively threw back his head and clenched his teeth. Blood leaked from his nose, mouth, and ears, running over his already melted skin.

While firing the Air Bullets, Klein had changed the soul he was Grazing.

It was still the Interrogator, and the power was still Psychic Piercing!

Against a Beyonder like a Zombie, whose body was as tough as steel, inflicting damage upon the Spirit Body was far more effective!

As the repeated strikes made Steel Maveti's body stiffen for brief intervals, he lost the restraints of reason. Black, rigid hair grew from his face strand by strand, while his teeth and nails continually lengthened. His skin squirmed in an attempt to knit together, changing from black to white. He resembled a genuine, crazed zombie thirsting for blood!

Klein seized the opportunity to close the distance. Bending low, he brushed past Steel Maveti's shoulder and stuffed the item in his hand into the hideous wound he had created in the man's body.

It was a bottle containing several ordinary brass bullets and a large quantity of Sun Holy Water that Klein had prepared in advance!

Oof!

Because Klein didn't stop moving, Squall's attempt to control him failed once more.

Pivoting on his heel, he moved behind Steel Maveti, his back to the pirate.

Pow! He raised his right hand and snapped his fingers before Steel Maveti could pull out the glass bottle.

Bang!

A muffled explosion rang out, and fiery crimson light soared upward, shattering the already fragile glass bottle. A great quantity of Sun Holy Water flooded Steel Maveti's body.

The infamous pirate with a bounty of 6,000 pounds froze in place, an anguished groan escaping his throat.

He dropped to his knees as dark green gas poured from his body and vanished into the air.

At this sight, Squall's eyes darkened. He immediately took out an iron-black charm and muttered a word in ancient Hermes: "Shriek!"

The charm abruptly cracked and vanished, completing the sacrifice.

An invisible sound wave swept outward like a tsunami, boring into the ears of everyone nearby almost simultaneously.

Klein's mind buzzed, and the tip of his nose turned cold. Blood oozed out. Blood Brambles Hendry, atop the Flying Carpet, was in an even more miserable state. His face twisted, and his body convulsed as he fell, unable even to emit a tragic scream. Blazing Danitz, who stood far away on a rooftop, was unaffected. Delighted, he hurled another highly compressed incandescent fireball.

The fireball struck Hendry's body without resistance and exploded with a thunderous boom.

Flesh and blood splattered outward. Flames enveloped his fingers, calves, and head before he fell to the ground. The Flying Carpet then lost control and slowly descended.

Squall took the opportunity to turn and flee without the slightest hesitation.

However, he was astonished to discover that the terrifying man who had nearly killed Steel Maveti within ten seconds didn't appear hindered in the least. He had turned and given chase!

How is that possible? That's a Shriek charm made by the Admiral! Squall had no time to consider the reason. He ran as fast as he could down the other street.

At that moment, his head was suddenly struck hard by someone. Tearing pain made his entire body spasm.

The lightning in Klein's eyes faded as he raised his right hand and snapped his fingers once more.

Bang!

An Air Bullet crossed the distance of roughly ten meters and accurately bored into the back of Squall's head.

Squall didn't possess Steel's inhuman endurance. His vision immediately went black, and he slowly collapsed.

Klein caught up in a few strides, extended his left hand, and allowed the jubilant Creeping Hunger to enjoy its meal.

I've heard the voice of the True Creator before, and I've experienced Mr. Door's cries for help. I have tremendous resistance in this respect and can even recover quickly from a Wraith's shriek, much less a mere charm... Klein muttered inwardly as he watched Squall's body be reduced to flesh and blood and plunge into Creeping Hunger's open mouth.

After a few seconds, Creeping Hunger came to a satisfied halt, and Klein swiftly returned to Steel Maveti's side.

The Zombie had been miserably purified. Although his head remained intact, he was already dead.

Klein stepped forward, picked up the peacock-blue Flying Carpet that had fallen to the ground, wrapped it around Steel's body, and hoisted it up.

Glancing back at 15 Amyris Avenue, he listened to the commotion caused by the rampaging zombies and puppets and sensed the approaching Mandated Punishers. Klein pulled the hood over his head and bowed slightly in their direction as a salute.

Pa!

Klein rubbed his fingers together, setting all the paper fragments around him ablaze and producing columns of crimson flame.

In the firelight, his body turned illusory, and he vanished from the spot, the items with him transforming into shadows.

As for the Beyonder characteristics of Squall and Hendry, which were slowly taking shape, he neither looked at nor paid any attention to them.

Greed would only draw him into unnecessary danger. Besides, the harvest he had already reaped was sufficient!

After leaving some Mandated Punishers behind to deal with the rampaging zombies and puppets, the pirates injured by their "companions," and the captured Beyonder adventurers, Cordoba Roye led the rest of the team over. All they found, however, were scattered corpses, scalpels, and withered trees in the streets. They could distinguish the fading aura of the sun and the evil sensation emanating from the depths of flesh and blood.

Chapter 528: Grazing

It was early morning, and the sky had only just begun to brighten. Most alleys remained dark and quiet.

With the aid of Danitz's cloak, Klein blended into the shadows and moved swiftly. His surroundings seemed grayish-black and transparent, while reality itself appeared blurred. Every sound seemed to come from the depths of the starry sky, hollow and distant.

After putting as much distance as possible between himself and Amyris Avenue, he found his bearings and "grew" out of the shadows. He appeared in an empty alley strewn with broken stones.

Klein shook his hands, casting Steel Maveti and the peacock-blue Flying Carpet before him. He wanted to complete the Grazing before Maveti's Spirit Body began to dissipate.

After discarding the Flying Carpet, he stepped forward, extended his left hand, and spread his five fingers over the still-warm corpse.

Creeping Hunger revealed its true appearance--a thin material that seemed to be made of human skin.

Two eyes split open in the center of its palm. Their pupils were bright red, as though dyed in blood.

Whoosh!

A bone-chilling wind suddenly swept through the alley. It swirled around Steel Maveti's corpse and twisted into an almost transparent human figure. One could vaguely make out thick lips and curls of hair resembling steel beads.

Maveti's Spirit Body wore an expression of abnormal pain as he desperately resisted Creeping Hunger's suction. Meanwhile, grayish-white and blackish-green motes of light surged like a galaxy, rapidly flying out of his body and merging into the transparent figure.

No!

Maveti emitted a soundless scream. It was already too late to beg for mercy. He could only be dragged desperately into the human-skin glove, becoming attached to an empty finger and revealing the Beyonder characteristic that connected with the environment to a certain extent.

This connection determined which powers would be available when Grazing Steel Maveti's soul. There might be one, two, or no more than three. Before the powers became fixed, Klein had no control over their number or nature. It was entirely random.

Creeping Hunger rapidly concealed its pallor and finally returned to its disguised state: an ordinary black glove.

Klein closed his eyes and sensed the changes for several seconds before releasing a deep sigh.

This time, his luck wasn't bad, but neither was it especially good.

He had successfully obtained three of Steel Maveti's Beyonder powers, but he hadn't received what he wanted most--a body as hard as steel, impervious to bullets, flames, and explosives beneath a certain level, coupled with tenacious vitality.

The first is a Zombie's power, which allows me to unleash a critical blow, compensating for my former weakness of having only a nimble fighting style. The second is mastery over ice. Although I can't condense an ice spear or create a blizzard like the Demoness pathway to launch a direct attack, I can freeze the surrounding ground, lower the air temperature, and effectively hinder an enemy's movements. If I make physical contact, I can even freeze the enemy's body or stiffen his flesh and blood. It complements my combat abilities perfectly. The third is the manipulation of zombies. Heh heh, I no longer need to worry about having no partners for a round of cards... Klein considered this briefly, then bent down to inspect the items on Steel Maveti's person.

He quickly found 26 pounds, 11 soli, and 8 pence in cash, as well as an assortment of strange objects that appeared ordinary, such as candles, clips, and a thorny whip.

Considering that Steel Maveti belonged to the Rose School of Thought and was the type of person who indulged his desires, Klein vaguely understood what was going on. He spat on the ground and took only the money.

Werewolves possess supernatural healing powers, while Zombies have bodies like steel. He truly had no need to carry medicine or items related to healing... It's just as Danitz concluded: Maveti doesn't possess any mystical items... That's right, mystical items are rare to begin with. Many have obvious flaws and negative effects. Not everyone can obtain one merely because they want one. Previously, among that group comprising a Wraith, a Zombie, and a Werewolf, there were only two mystical items--the Scarlet Lunar Corona and the Biological Poison Bottle... Klein nodded and took out a folded sheet of paper.

He unfolded the yellowed paper and placed it over Steel Maveti's face.

It was a bounty notice bearing Maveti's portrait and the corresponding reward: "6,000 pounds!"

Klein straightened, took a step back, stepped onto the peacock-blue carpet, and pulled out a paper figurine.

Pa!

With a swing of his arm and a flick of his wrist, he threw out the paper figurine. It burst into flame before scattering into ash.

Immediately afterward, he melted into shadow and vanished from the alley along with the items.

Two or three minutes later, Cordoba Roye rushed over with part of the Mandated Punisher team, just in time to see a cool breeze lift the bounty notice and reveal Steel Maveti's dissolving flesh and blood and his wide-open eyes.

Whoosh. The bounty notice fell to one side, still facing upward.

Having lost all trace of their target, Cordoba and the others silently carried Steel's corpse back to the site of the battle.

Glancing at the team members examining the scene, Deacon Cordoba restrained his emotions and asked in a deep voice, "A believer of the Sun?"

This was the most important question for him and his diocese.

The Mandated Punisher responsible for using supernatural means to find clues and reconstruct the truth wasn't a Beyonder of the Storm pathway, but a Sequence 7 Knowledge Keeper of the Reader pathway, also known as a Detective. He didn't answer immediately. Instead, he walked over and crouched to examine Maveti's corpse.

After a while, he looked up and said, "Not a believer of the Sun. It was probably the result of a mystical item's powers."

"Why?" Cordoba asked with a frown.

The Mandated Punisher said, "There is an evil sensation here that stems from flesh and blood. One of the corpses was devoured in that manner, leaving only traces of the body and its possessions. If it were a Sun believer, he wouldn't do such a thing even with the aid of an item. To them, anything in the True Creator's domain is sacrilegious and requires immediate purification or sealing. They would be unlikely to borrow its power."

After thinking for several seconds, Cordoba agreed with the conclusion and asked, "Someone from the Aurora Order?"

"No. A true Rose Bishop or Shepherd wouldn't act so crudely. They would certainly wrap the corpse within their body and deal with the scene after leaving. There's no reason to dismember the body on the spot. It wastes time and leaves behind a collection of objects that could be considered valuable. Devouring him in such haste resembles the negative effect of a mystical item," the Knowledge Keeper said calmly.

"A mystical item possessing powers in both the Sun and Secrets Suppliant domains? Perhaps something with abilities like a Shepherd's? Berg, what's your opinion?" As a deacon, Cordoba had sufficient clearance to know about Shepherds and their special abilities. The Knowledge Keeper knew because of his position in the team. The other Mandated Punishers had no idea what they were discussing.

Knowledge Keeper Berg stood.

"All are possible."

"Any suspects?" Cordoba asked in a low voice.

Berg shook his head.

"There are no suspects for the time being.

"But one thing can be confirmed. Blood Brambles was killed by Blazing Danitz. The other person is his helper, a terrifying powerhouse no weaker than a Pirate Admiral."

"A terrifying powerhouse..." Cordoba's expression turned grave as he muttered under his breath. Then his eyes lit up. "Could it be Edwina? She can simulate certain Beyonder powers she has witnessed before!"

Knowledge Keeper Berg pondered for a moment before saying, "We can't rule out that possibility, but our intelligence indicates that she was near Sonia Island a few days ago.

"Unless--unless she can simulate a corresponding ability and use the spirit world to travel here."

Cordoba paced back and forth, looked around, and said, "Handle the scene, and don't draw the nearby residents' attention to it.

"I'll submit the mission report. Let us hope we receive more useful information."

This involved a powerhouse whose combat ability was at least equivalent to Sequence 5. He could only restrain his irascible temper and make the most rational decision.

Inside Old Quinn's house, in the basement sealed by a heavy stone door.
