Alger sat on the low bed, quietly watching the candles on the table, when his vision blurred. He saw gray fog emanate, along with The Fool seated in a high-backed chair, looking down upon everything.

A blurred silhouette appeared below, hands clasped before his face as he said piously, "Honorable Mr. Fool, the matter has been successfully concluded."

The matter has been successfully concluded? This involved Steel Maveti and several other Beyonders... The World is certainly efficient. His strength surpasses what I expected. As expected of Mr. Fool's Blessed... Heh heh, he may be able to sell another Beyonder characteristic at the Tarot Club, perhaps even more than one... How did he evade the effects of Sealed Artifact 2-37? Is it something unique to a god's Blessed? A thought flashed through Alger's mind, and he instinctively thanked Mr. Fool.

Then he took out a small metal bottle and smeared mint extract beneath his nose.

The strong, pungent odor surged into his head, instantly leaving Alger exceptionally clearheaded.

At some point, a white gauze mask had appeared in his hands, and he poured Full Moon Essence Oil onto it.

After methodically putting on the mask, Alger rose and slowly approached the heavy stone door.

Moving methodically, he retrieved a sealed metal canister from inside his clothes, twisted the mechanical switch, and pulled out a tube.

Then he pushed the tube through a crack in the stone door and extended it outside.

Without a sound, the gas in the metal container began to escape.

This was the Sanguine anesthetic gas provided by The Moon, Emlyn!

It could knock out strong ordinary people, Sequence 9 Beyonders with robust bodies, and even higher-Sequence Beyonders who lacked physical resilience!

Even if Old Quinn can resist the gas, he will still be significantly affected. As for his helpers and friends, heh... Alger leaned against the stone door and waited patiently.

When nearly a third of the gas had dispersed from the canister, he withdrew the tube and closed the switch.

He turned and listened attentively, receiving a satisfactory answer.

Alger revealed a faint smile. He rapidly drew in his aura and compressed it within himself.

Suddenly, the muscles across his body bulged, and he punched forward.

Raging Blow!

Bang!

The iron lock on the heavy stone door shattered, and the door itself swung open.

Alger withdrew his fist and walked out, leaving only sleeping figures behind.

Author's Note: Creeping Hunger has two modes, Grazing and Devouring. The former targets the soul and Beyonder characteristic, while the latter targets flesh and blood.

Chapter 529: Tacit Understanding

Outside the Wind of Azure Inn at 48 Acid Lemon Street.

Dressed in a thick jacket and pantaloons, Blazing Danitz returned by a circuitous route. He pressed himself against the corner of a wall and remained motionless for a long time, hesitating over whether to seize the opportunity to flee and never again face the terrifying madman Gehrman Sparrow.

Unlike the battle at Bansy Harbor, this time he had hidden on a distant rooftop, allowing him to take in the entire battlefield. Thus, while supporting Gehrman Sparrow and fighting Blood Brambles Hendry, he had finally witnessed the crazy adventurer's combat methods and gained a grasp of his Beyonder powers and traits.

Paper Figurine Substitutes, traveling through flames, psychic attacks, a holy beam from the Sun domain, firing Air Bullets with his fingers, the ability to disguise himself as another person, and a glove that changed appearance several times and devoured Squall. His powers far exceed my imagination, and several of them are incompatible with one another. This can't be explained simply by adding one or two mystical items to a particular Sequence... Furthermore, that sense of hunger from the depths of my soul explains something... That glove belonging to Vice Admiral Hurricane--Creeping Hunger! Captain specifically warned us that the glove can Graze the souls of many Beyonders and use their powers... Danitz reviewed his earlier thoughts and reached a final conclusion.

Gehrman Sparrow is Creeping Hunger's present owner!

He didn't feel the slightest contempt simply because Gehrman had relied on a powerful mystical item to display such terrifying strength. Instead, he felt even greater reverence for him.

There were two reasons. First, using a mystical item to its full potential depended largely on the wielder's abilities. Without sufficient strength and extraordinary combat experience, even someone with Creeping Hunger couldn't have finished Steel Maveti and Calm Squall within ten seconds.

Second, his Captain, Vice Admiral Edwina Edwards, had learned from secret sources that Vice Admiral Hurricane hadn't died at the hands of the Church of Storms. Instead, he had been swiftly slain by a particular powerhouse while fleeing.

Danitz understood perfectly well how powerful Vice Admiral Hurricane Qilangos had been at his peak. He knew that a powerhouse capable of easily finishing him off would stand among the mightiest pirates. Indeed, such a person would rank with the two most powerful and terrifying figures, the King of the Five Seas and Queen Mystic!

Although surprise played a role, that would lower the assessment only slightly. The person wouldn't be much weaker than the King of Immortality and would be stronger than Admiral Hell and the Admiral of Blood... Creeping Hunger now belongs to Gehrman Sparrow. That means either he killed Qilangos, or a powerful figure on the level of the Four Kings stands behind him. Either possibility is far more terrifying than I originally expected! Danitz's body tensed. He truly didn't wish to face the adventurer with mad blood flowing through his veins.

Phew... He exhaled. He felt he had hesitated there too long and needed to decide at once.

Gehrman Sparrow knows divination, and he's quite confident in his abilities in that domain. With my Shadow Cloak in his possession, attempting to escape will not only fail but also infuriate him... The Shadow Cloak is a rare mystical item... Gritting his teeth, Danitz turned the corner, entered the inn, and went all the way to the luxurious suite.

After waiting and observing cautiously for several seconds, Danitz took out his key and opened the door.

The room was dark, the gas wall lamps unlit. The dim light of early morning shone through the window and fell upon Gehrman Sparrow, who faced the door.

The adventurer had changed back into his usual black tweed coat and dark trousers. He held a half top hat in one hand, with his right ankle resting on his left thigh.

He leaned back slightly, his face obscured because the window was behind him. Only his exceptionally clear dark brown eyes watched the door with indifference and composure.

Danitz unconsciously lowered his head, laughed dryly, and said, "Following your instructions, I went around to several news agencies, wrote the information that Blazing killed Steel Maveti, Blood Brambles Hendry, and Squall on pieces of paper, and threw them inside.

"Of course, I mentioned that he had a powerful helper--a mysterious and unknown top adventurer and veteran bounty hunter."

Klein nodded and revealed a courteous smile.

"Very good."

With a sigh of relief, Danitz looked around and saw the peacock-blue carpet.

He stared blankly for several seconds before asking in puzzlement, "What about Steel's and Hendry's heads?"

Klein calmly replied, "I didn't take them."

"You didn't take them?" Danitz exclaimed in astonishment. "Then what about our bounty?"

As long as they possessed Steel's and Blood Brambles' heads, he could collect the corresponding bounties through people with connections. Although doing so would cost them fifteen to thirty percent of the proceeds, as a pirate he couldn't claim the bounties himself. This was the only way. The military and Church used bounties to encourage pirates to kill one another, but that didn't mean they would turn a blind eye to a gift that came walking through their door.

To Danitz's surprise, Gehrman Sparrow, whose eyes appeared crazed with a thirst for money, had actually failed to take Steel's and Blood Brambles' heads. That meant he had voluntarily abandoned the bounty.

Klein didn't answer. He pointed to the Flying Carpet on the floor.

"You helped with this. You have two choices.

"One is 3,000 pounds in cash. The other is this Flying Carpet.

"You have five seconds to decide. Exceeding the time limit will count as forfeiture."

Three thousand pounds in cash or the Flying Carpet? This Flying Carpet appears capable only of hovering and flying. Furthermore, it isn't very fast, so it isn't particularly useful... No, at sea, for a Beyonder who doesn't belong to the Sailor pathway, such an item is very precious. At the very least, there would be no need to worry about what happens after a ship sinks... Danitz was momentarily caught in a dilemma.

Then he heard Gehrman Sparrow begin counting.

"Three..."

Danitz's heart skipped a beat, and he hurriedly said, "The Flying Carpet!"

Klein nodded. "All right."

Knowing that Gehrman possessed great credibility, Danitz finally relaxed. Then he grumbled, "Why didn't you count five and four?"

Wasn't it supposed to be five seconds!?

Without changing his expression, Klein replied, "I counted them inwardly."

Counted inwardly... Danitz drew a deep breath and grimaced.

Klein slowly rose, flicked his wrist, and tossed the hat from his hand. It landed securely on the rack, and he walked toward his bedroom.

Cathedral of Waves.

Entering under the pretext of attending morning prayers, Alger met the diocesan bishop Chogo and recounted everything that had happened the previous night. The only alteration was that the Sanguine anesthetic gas became a Slumber Charm from the Church of Evernight.

Chogo nodded approvingly.

"To venture deep into the enemy's lair and risk death for the sake of your faith is an act worthy of praise. You are a true attendant of the Storm.

"Last night, we captured five Beyonders and killed two on the spot. As a result of this operation, Maveti, Hendry, and Squall are also dead. The heads of those three pirates will either never appear or will be in our possession, so the government bounties will belong to us.

"The total reward will exceed 10,000 pounds, and you will receive the largest share: 6,000 pounds.

"Do not refuse. The Lord has told us that those who fight for their faith need not be modest."

"Holy Lord of Storms!" An excited Alger pounded his left chest with his fist.

Although he and The World hadn't agreed on how to divide the spoils, he believed they had reached a tacit understanding. For instance, they weren't to interfere with each other's gains when hunting pirates. Since the bounty had to be laundered through the Church, they would split it equally.

As for bounties offered by countries and organizations such as Intis and Feysac, Alger held no expectations. Every time a bounty was claimed, the claimant had to surrender the target's corpse or head. Therefore, only one party could be chosen. Only those with powerful backgrounds and good relations with various countries and organizations could collect them all.

Three thousand pounds will belong to The World... If I lose 3,000 pounds all at once, it will arouse suspicion. Yes, I'll have to find an opportunity to purchase a mystical item. Such things are rare and therefore often overpriced, especially in times of urgent need. No one will doubt that I spent 5,000 pounds on an item worth 3,000... If I sell the items obtained from Old Quinn, including those he had received, the accounts will balance... Alger quickly devised a plan for what came next.

Knowing that 3,000 pounds awaited him, Klein sat happily in The Fool's high-backed chair and contemplated a serious question.

After Grazing Steel Maveti, he had to release one of Creeping Hunger's souls, as he had promised.

Their Beyonder powers are all quite useful. It would be a pity to release one before finding a replacement. Yes... Besides, I already released that Faceless. There doesn't seem to be a need to do so this time... Hesitation and reluctance stirred in Klein's heart, throwing him into a dilemma. He couldn't reach a decision.

After a long while, he leaned back and exhaled.

I can't deceive myself. He shook his head and laughed, relaxing once he had made up his mind.

He decided to keep his promise and release a soul.

The previous Faceless had been released ahead of time because he wanted to obtain corresponding information. That had already constituted an equal exchange.

Which one should I release? Miss Justice purchased the materials for the Psychiatrist potion and a mystical item worth 5,500 pounds. Even if she's wealthy, her finances should be strained, so it wouldn't be appropriate to sell the Psychiatrist characteristic at this time. Yes, she still owes my Blessed 2,000 pounds and will repay the money in February or March... Klein rejected that option and decided to release the Nightmare.

As a former Nighthawk, he had always felt an affinity and gratitude toward Beyonders of that domain based on his impressions and emotions. Therefore, with no other factors to consider, the choice wasn't difficult.

After calming himself, Klein picked up the human-skin glove he had previously brought above the gray fog. He closed his eyes and began sensing the distorted souls.

Without hesitation, he released the Nightmare.

Chapter 530: Chronicles

The Nightmare appeared beside the long bronze table.

He was a man in his thirties with black hair, blue eyes, a long, thin face, and prominent nasolabial folds. A beard that wasn't especially thick covered the area around his mouth and chin.

As the pain and distortion subsided, he pressed a hand to his chest and bowed solemnly.

Compared to the previous Faceless, the Nightmare was plainly less muddle-headed and weak after being released. It was unclear whether the Evernight pathway strengthened the soul more than the Seer pathway did, or whether it was because the Grazing had occurred only recently.

Klein sighed inwardly and began questioning him through spirit channeling. "Why did you die at Qilangos's hands?"

The corner of the Nightmare's mouth curled slightly, revealing a bitter smile.

"I'm a Red Glove of the Nighthawks. I was tracking a collection of ancient chronicles originating from a Balam imperial tomb. We suspected they were connected to the reason for Death's passing.

"I discovered that some of them might have fallen into the hands of a certain tycoon, so I led two companions aboard the ship he was traveling on. Unfortunately, just as we were about to begin our investigation, Qilangos's fleet attacked us."

"What happened to your companions?" Klein subconsciously asked.

The Nightmare said in a slightly pained voice, "Through our skillful teamwork, we originally had the chance to escape and even kill Qilangos, but our ship sank, forcing us into a lifeboat. Everyone, everyone died. No--they were killed in the line of duty!"

May the Goddess bless you... Klein inwardly drew a crimson moon.

This made him even more aware of the enormous difference between fighting at sea and fighting on land--environmental factors were critically important.

Beyonders who didn't belong to the Sailor pathway were at a tremendous disadvantage!

Had the Flying Carpet not been so large, cumbersome, and slow, making him an easy target, Klein would have chosen to keep it and give Danitz the money.

Fortunately, I have a murloc's bladder. I can find an Artisan to turn it into a mystical item that allows me to swim underwater... Unfortunately, Artisans are very difficult to find. Most belong to the Church of the God of Steam and Machinery, and few are unaffiliated. Otherwise, mystical items wouldn't be so rare. If worst comes to worst, I'll ask Mr. Hanged Man for help... Klein looked at the Nightmare who had died in the line of duty and leaned back before asking softly, "What is your name? What unfulfilled wishes do you have?"

The Nightmare's figure was already slowly dissipating. Hearing Klein's question, he smiled and said, "My name is Davy Raymond. I lost my parents, wife, and siblings in an incident involving black magic, leaving me with only one daughter, Neelu. She was born in 1330 and is a very lovely girl. I'm ashamed to say that, in order to investigate the truth behind the black magic incident, I joined the Nighthawks and later became a Red Glove. I failed to spend time with her, causing her to lose her father as well as her mother.

"Heh heh, I believe the Church will certainly give her my pension and secretly provide her with a certain amount of assistance. I'm not worried about her livelihood. I only hope to see her marry under the Goddess's witness, have a family of her own, and no longer be lonely."

"It's already 1350. She may already have someone she intends to marry," Klein said with a wistful sigh.

"Time flies..." Davy Raymond murmured in response. "Tell her that all the culprits have been punished and that I died in an accident. She no longer needs to hate anyone. Tell her Daddy loves her and that Daddy is very sorry..."

His figure grew increasingly transparent and was about to vanish.

Klein closed his eyes and asked, "Where does she live?

"What is the name of the tycoon who collected Death's chronicles?"

"We lived in Conant City, Desi County. It's a beautiful seaside city, with abundant rubber plantations nearby. If she hasn't moved, she'll be living at 67 Red Indus Street. The tycoon's name is Jimmy Necker. He probably fell into Qilangos's hands as well..." Before Davy Raymond could finish, his figure completely disappeared, leaving only a deep black, gem-like object on the surface of the glove.

Klein quietly watched the scene. After several seconds, he raised his right hand and tapped his chest four times clockwise. Then he said in a deep voice, "If I have the opportunity, I'll visit your daughter and see how she is doing."

In the palace that resembled a giant's residence, the fog was endless, eternally unchanging, without the slightest ripple.

Klein put away Davy Raymond's Beyonder characteristic, rubbed his forehead, and turned his attention back to Death's chronicles.

The Death pathway and the Evernight pathway are interchangeable at the higher Sequences. There's nothing strange about the Church sending personnel to investigate the relevant chronicles... I wonder whether Jimmy Necker is dead. I'll need to investigate... If he has already perished in some tragic incident and the item fell into Qilangos's hands, I'll have to investigate from the pirates' side... Qilangos's crew now belongs to Vice Admiral Ailment, Tracy...

Since it involves Death, it might be of some help to Mr. Azik. Klein decided to investigate it in passing. If he encountered any difficulties, he could write directly to the powerhouse.

After clearing his thoughts, he quickly returned to the real world and used the ritual to summon Creeping Hunger back.

Once everything was done, he saw that it was already too late to catch up on sleep and abandoned the idea. He decided to summarize the experiences and lessons from the previous night's operation.

The greatest lesson Klein had learned was that he had overestimated the Mandated Punishers' patience.

He had originally hoped that, even if something unexpected happened at the beginning, the Mandated Punishers would restrain themselves and wait for the true target, the main quarry, Steel Maveti, to appear.

When that happened, they would definitely use the Sealed Artifact capable of drawing many people into a dream, restraining all the unaffiliated Beyonders who had involved themselves in the assault. As for me, because of my uniqueness, I'd be able to realize that I was dreaming and forcibly break free. Then I could calmly deal with Steel Maveti and his helpers, achieving an instant kill and carrying away two corpses... Yet the moment the incident began, those irascible fellows charged inside. They didn't even leave a backup team to guard against contingencies. At most, they had the wielder of the Sealed Artifact remain at the perimeter with one or two guards... The more Klein thought about it, the less he knew whether to laugh or cry.

If Steel Maveti's group of pirates hadn't been comparatively cautious and assigned at least two Beyonders, together with all their puppets and zombies, to obstruct any Mandated Punishers who pursued them, Klein would probably have found himself in a two-against-five situation. Had that happened, he would have chosen to abandon the plan and make a strategic retreat.

What satisfied Klein most was that he had done quite well in making targeted preparations.

This was the professional habit of a Magician!

Because he had long ago learned the traits of the Mutant pathway's first five Sequences from Miss Sharron and Maric, Klein knew perfectly well that Steel Maveti, having passed through the Lunatic stage, could use irrational factors to resist Beyonder interference and influences on his mind. He possessed very strong resistance in that regard. Therefore, Klein abandoned the idea of using the Psychiatrist's Dragon Might and Frenzy, as well as the Nightmare, which was clearly weaker than the Mandated Punishers' Sealed Artifact. Instead, he focused on the Interrogator's Psychic Piercing and the Priest of Light's purification powers.

Psychic Piercing wasn't a Beyonder power that disrupted thoughts and affected the soul, but a means of directly attacking the Spirit Body. In essence, the other effects acted upon the Body of Heart and Mind, while this targeted the Soul Body. There was a clear distinction between them.

If any part of that combination had failed to connect, Steel Maveti would have recovered, making it impossible for me to kill him quickly. In that environment, that would have meant failure. Yes... I also exploited the pathway's trait of surrendering to desire, which makes their emotions easy to grasp. I believed that once they suffered a psychic attack, there was a high probability they would madly counterattack without considering anything else...

In fact, the safest way to deal with a Zombie would be to arrange matchsticks in advance or bring Danitz. I could use Flaming Jump and Light of Holiness to kite Maveti. He would want to fight but be completely unable to reach me. He couldn't escape even if he wanted to. Danitz would only need to send over a fireball, and I could catch up with him. Unfortunately, that would waste too much time, and the circumstances didn't permit it...

Klein sighed, took out his pocket watch, snapped it open, and checked the time.

Seeing that it was nearly nine in the morning, he turned the door handle and walked out of the bedroom.

At that moment, Danitz lay in a reclining chair, snoring like a steam engine operating in the room.

He was quite vigilant, opening his eyes and sitting up the moment Klein emerged.

"Are you going out?" Danitz asked when he saw Gehrman Sparrow remove his hat from the coat rack.

"Yes." Klein maintained his persona and didn't explain that he intended to act by helping someone confess his love.

What about me? Steel Maveti and almost all his men are dead, so I don't have to worry about them anymore... Even if the newspapers don't publish the news, I can spread it myself. Idle pirates and adventurers will use it as material for boasting, spreading the matter across the sea until Captain hears of it. Those dogshit fellows can't do anything but drink and brag, but even so, they still have their uses... Danitz thought for a moment before asking with hidden apprehension, "Mr. Sparrow, can I--can I leave now?"

Klein revealed a faint smile.

"You have always been free."

That's right... He didn't capture me this time. I was merely seeking his help... I've always been free! Danitz was stunned for a moment before delight welled within him.

But at that instant, he heard Gehrman Sparrow's voice drift lightly toward him.

"But that ends now."

Huh? What? Danitz looked confused.

It took him no fewer than three seconds to understand what Gehrman Sparrow meant.

He had been captured again!

"Why?" Danitz demanded, both angry and aggrieved.

Klein put on his hat and said in a low voice, "I want to meet your Captain."

Danitz's eyes widened. He abruptly stood and shouted, "What do you want?"

This fellow is a little too agitated... Klein calmly replied, "I have some questions for her."

Chapter 531: Great Acting

He has some questions for Captain?

Danitz frowned as he watched Gehrman Sparrow leave the room, unsure what his real motive was.

What is this fellow really after? Money? Riches? He had time to take the heads of Steel Maveti and Blood Brambles Hendry and exchange them for nearly 10,000 pounds in bounties. Yes, even after paying the commissions, it would still amount to seven or eight thousand pounds. Yet he didn't do so. Moreover, he generously shared the spoils of war with me. This is very contradictory. When he first heard that I was Blazing, his first reaction was to call me a 3,000-pound bounty... In Captain's words, this isn't scientific...

Why would he deliberately give up the bounty? Unless... Unless he has a better way to claim it safely, or he deliberately left it for someone? Yes... He had been prepared for the Mandated Punishers' appearance... H-he has his own connections and sources of information! That explains everything!

There's also the demigod who might have killed Qilangos. Hmm, there's a powerful, secretive organization behind Gehrman Sparrow!

Shocked by his own conjecture, Danitz instinctively tried to use his body language to express his feelings, but he forgot that his left arm was still fractured. He immediately grimaced in pain.

This made him feel even more terrified. He didn't want his captain to meet such a dangerous and crazy fellow.

He even suspected that Gehrman Sparrow's real motive was his captain's 26,000-pound bounty!

In a duel, that lunatic might not necessarily beat Captain, and there are still the first mate, second mate, third mate, and several boatswains. But he has a terrifying organization backing him! You want to hurt Captain? Over my dead body! Danitz puffed out his chest, raised his chin, and was filled with a stirring sense of self-sacrifice.

He ran a hand through his hair, gave a long grunt, and said to himself, "I'll make further observations. Perhaps Gehrman Sparrow really only has questions that he wants answered."

Subconsciously, he looked around and found both the Flying Carpet and the Shadow Cloak still there.

In other words, Gehrman Sparrow left my belongings behind. Isn't he afraid that I'll take the opportunity to escape? Or did he steal one of my hairs to make it easier to divine my whereabouts?

No, no, he's a crazy guy. Even if he wishes to use my hair, he would just walk over and extend his hand to pull one out. It's impossible for him to do it secretly... Right, there's a powerful and secretive organization behind him! Perhaps, at this moment, there are people from that organization secretly monitoring me. They're waiting for me to leave, waiting for me to seek out Captain... How insidious!

Gehrman Sparrow must've gone out on purpose! Danitz felt that, thanks to his experience and wisdom, he had grasped the truth of the matter.

He paced back and forth before sitting down again in the reclining chair. Then, with a silent, mocking smile, he thought:

I'm not leaving!

I want to see what you can do!

I will not bring danger to Captain!

At a quarter past three in the afternoon, on Symeem Island.

This island was also part of the Rorsted Archipelago, but it was the farthest from the City of Generosity, Bayam. It took nearly five hours to reach by liner.

On the way, Klein bought a local-style suit and a small suitcase to hold the clothes he changed out of, for a total of fourteen soli. Together, they didn't even cost a pound.

It's really cheap. It's mere change compared to the price of a complete suit... Wearing pantaloons, a thick brown jacket, and a light brown cap, Klein disembarked from the liner with an inconspicuous native face and entered one of Symeem Island's old ports. He had spent time buying clothes and changing his appearance, causing him to miss the nine o'clock ferry and leaving him with no choice but to take the one at ten.

Thinking of the expenses he had incurred for this performance, he couldn't help but calculate his current financial situation.

The Faceless characteristic sold for 3,825 pounds. The pirate bounties netted me 3,000 pounds. Although I haven't received the 6,825 pounds yet, I can basically include it in my budget as long as Mr. Hanged Man isn't exposed...

The cash left over from Donna and company's gratuity adds up to 255 pounds in bits and pieces...

I found 26 pounds, 11 soli, and 8 pence from Steel Maveti's body...

Yes, there are also the five gold coins in reserve...

I haven't spent much money recently, only a little more than a pound in total. That's something to be happy about...

With that, I'll have 7,110 pounds. Moreover, I still have the Nightmare Beyonder characteristic and the murloc's bladder. They're still worth something.

On second thought, I earned a 3,000-pound bounty along with a Zombie Beyonder characteristic. The latter is worth about 3,000 to 5,000 pounds. That is to say, I earned about 7,000 pounds... This is only from Steel Maveti, Blood Brambles Hendry, and Squall. Besides, quite a lot went to waste... Indeed, hunting pirates is quite a good job. It allows me to administer justice, punish evil, protect the weak and innocent, and become rich overnight...

Klein subconsciously turned his head to the side, only to see that the seawater was much clearer than Bayam's. It was like a huge, sparkling green gem reflecting golden rays in the sunlight.

Sure enough, there is a reason generations of adventurers have gone to sea in search of wealth. Even if I deduct the cost of the remnant spirituality of ancient wraiths, eyes from a six-winged gargoyle, spring water from Sonia Island's Golden Spring, and the other supplementary ingredients for a Marionettist, I'll still have enough money left to buy a decent manor here, in Desi Bay, in the Southern Continent's colonies, or in a nonmetropolitan area... Loen's countryside is said to be very beautiful, and if it turns out that I'm truly unable to return, I can consider settling down in a similar place... Yes, I still have a 10% stake in the Backlund Bike Company, and my future returns won't be low... Filled with confidence, Klein straightened his back and thought about the future.

After letting his thoughts wander, Klein began to think about the more realistic question, which was whether he should sell the Nightmare Beyonder characteristic, find an opportunity to return it to the Church of Evernight, or create a mystical item with it through an Artisan.

It depends on the situation. The ideal solution would be to sell it to the Church... The martyr, Klein, who had taken two potions from the Nighthawks, thought with uncertainty.

At the same time, he hoped that Little Sun would soon reach Sequence 7 and be qualified to gain clearance to the methods used to remove the mental corruption from a Beyonder characteristic.

As for releasing the Priest of Light from the glove, he was in no hurry. Little Sun had only just received the Sequence 7 potion formula, so he had no need for it at the moment. He would have to wait a long time, and the powers of a Priest of Light were extremely effective against a pirate like Admiral of Blood--far more so than the Sun Brooch.

As his thoughts slowly settled down, Klein entered the small port town.

The place was basically filled with natives. Their skin was nearly bronze, and their black hair had slight, natural curls. Their bodies emitted a scent that was derived from prolonged contact with spices.

After changing his appearance and asking about Raine's situation and whether there was any news of Wendt's death, Klein found a secluded corner. He wiped his face with his hand, transforming into the tall, thin Wendt, whose facial features were rather distinctive.

He carried his suitcase and circled around the town to its border. It was then that he saw the winery run by Raine's family.

The flaxen-haired girl could no longer be called a young girl. She had clearly matured a great deal since Wendt last remembered her.

She was sweeping the entrance, and there was no one around.

Phew... Klein took a deep breath and slowly let it out, feeling as though he had been placed in a very difficult position.

As an experienced keyboard warrior, he knew of theories like method acting and experiential acting, but his understanding went no deeper. He could only try to imagine Wendt's feelings and how he would behave in this situation.

Finally, he closed his eyes and walked over.

Raine looked up when she heard footsteps, finally identifying the visitor.

She opened her mouth slightly and let out a half-surprised exclamation before saying with a straight face, "Why are you suddenly back?"

Remember, you're just acting... Klein revealed a smile.

"I came to bid you farewell."

He spoke in a local dialect with a faint Bayam accent.

The language of the Rorsted Archipelago was also derived from ancient Feysac and constituted another variant of it. As half a historian, Klein could master it easily, and doing so had taken him only a short time.

"Farewell?" Raine asked, slightly surprised.

Klein turned his head, looked to the side, and smiled.

"I'm going to chase after a treasure trove, and I don't know when I'll be able to come back.

"When the time comes, I'll return with a great deal of money. I'll buy a manor in the suburbs, plant some rubber trees, set up a vineyard, have my own mill, wine cellar, and blacksmith shop, and fill the air with the scent of all kinds of spices. Then, I'll buy a few slaves and hire some servants, just like those masters. Heh heh, but I'll still be short of one thing."

Suppressing the goosebumps that threatened to rise on his skin, he turned and looked Raine in the eye.

"I still lack a wife and a mistress for the manor.

"Raine, I like you. I wish to marry you. I'm not saying it today to get an answer, but because I'm afraid I might never have another chance to tell you."

After listening quietly, Raine suddenly shouted, "Wendt, you're a coward!"

Ah... This reaction isn't right... Klein deliberately revealed a surprised expression.

Raine suppressed her voice and said, "Three years ago, three freaking years ago, I was already prepared to head to Bayam with you. Yet, you didn't say anything in the end! You coward! You chicken!

"What's the use of telling me now? You're going to sea soon, and you may never come back!"

The more she spoke, the more agitated she became.

"You've said it out loud, so now you're happy. You have no regrets, but what about me? I'll have to keep waiting for you to return and live on in pain? You selfish bastard!"

She waved the broom in her hand and swung it towards Wendt.

Klein knew that the real Wendt would have knocked the broom aside, hugged the girl, and told her that he wasn't going out to sea again, but he couldn't do the same. He had to let himself be chased away in disgrace, running all the way to a nearby alley. There, he struck his head against the wall while silently cursing himself.

It was too f**king awkward!

This is too f**king awkward!

Raine went back to the door, picked up a broom, and crouched down.

Her face was ashen, and no one knew what she was thinking.

Vaguely, she heard a sound, and she fell asleep.

After using the charm, Klein turned around and nudged Raine, who sat on the ground leaning against the wall. Then he quickly left the area and hid at a distance to observe the results.

Raine soon awoke and realized that she had fallen asleep without noticing. Everything that had just happened seemed like a dream.

She remained sitting there, motionless for quite a while.

Suddenly, she lowered her head and let out a sharp curse that seemed to come from the depths of her throat.

"Wendt, you selfish bastard!"

Sigh... Feeling the faintest sensation of his potion digesting, Klein changed his appearance and left.

He would have to spend the night on Symeem Island, as there were no liners headed for Bayam until the morning.

Chapter 532: Lord "Caucasian"

After changing his face and turning onto one of the two main streets, Klein went straight to the green mailbox and took a letter he had prepared long ago from his pocket.

This was a "death notice" he had forged in imitation of an official police document. It was addressed to the sergeant of Symeem Harbor and concerned the sudden death in Bayam of a local resident, Wendt.

When deciding to do the acting, Klein had worked out a plan to keep things on track and not cause irreparable damage to the girl, Raine.

His plan was to use the Slumber Charm to make the actual performance seem like a dream. This way, if Raine had no love for Wendt, she would reject his confession outright. After hearing of Wendt's death, she wouldn't feel guilty, only somewhat frightened at most. Going to church to pray and confess would effectively put her at ease.

If Raine also liked Wendt and responded to the confession, then the dream would give Klein a chance to extricate himself from the situation. The notification of Wendt's death would put an end to Raine's hopes without having too negative an impact on her future life.

But even so, it's still a bit cruel. No matter what kind of woman she is, having a man confess to her in a dream after his death is bound to be heartbreaking. She won't be able to recover for a long time.

Well... If I hadn't done anything, Raine would definitely have felt very sad when the news of Wendt's death reached her, even if it might not have been as serious. However, the knot in her heart would never have been untied, and she would have spent the rest of her life wondering whether Wendt had gone adventuring to secure a future for them both, or if he simply didn't like her...

Ending things this way isn't so bad. Once she has overcome her pain, she will go through life with the comfort of knowing that she was once truly loved.

Sigh... Regardless, forcibly interfering in others' lives for the sake of acting and affecting an innocent person's life to some degree, even under the pretext of fulfilling a wish, isn't truly kind. Just as Roselle said, the farther one advances along a Beyonder pathway, the more twisted and evil it feels. The acting method might even be a catalyst... All I can do is try my best to minimize such effects... After mailing the letter, Klein exhaled and, wearing an inconspicuous native face, walked to the only inn in town.

Along the way, he summarized his earlier experience. "Disguising yourself as someone else and obtaining feedback" was likely a central tenet of the Faceless principles, second only to "you can disguise yourself as anyone, but you are ultimately yourself."

If it were any other Faceless, they would've suppressed the news of Wendt's death for the sake of the role. They would have accepted Raine's feelings, spent a year or two in a relationship with her, married her, and had children with her. Then, to avoid being bound by the identity's various relationships, they would remember who they truly were and leave coldly... If they weren't exposed during the process, most of the potion would be digested... But I really can't do it! It's completely against my conscience! I can only try to push the limits... Klein sighed, inexplicably afraid.

He shook his head and thought with silent self-deprecation, Beyonders not only have to constantly fight threats and madness, but also all kinds of evil thoughts and the corruption that drags them down and can make them fall if they aren't careful...

Even so, at the end of the day, one might still be corrupted by the abyss, becoming a monster that one swears to get rid of. Sigh...

Suppressing his thoughts, Klein stepped into the inn and said to the boss behind the counter, "An ordinary room."

The thin boss glanced up and said, "A valid ID certificate."

How can a face I just came up with have one? Klein smiled in embarrassment.

"I forgot to bring it."

"Then you cannot stay here. That's the rule in our town." The boss lowered his head again and continued calculating the day's income and expenses.

Klein took out a soli note and pushed it over as if nothing had happened.

The boss's eyes suddenly widened.

"No, no, put it away! I don't want to be locked up by the sergeant!

"Get out, get out, you dirty bastard with no proof of identity."

Klein was thrown out of the inn in a daze, unable to believe that the omnipotent entity known as money had just lost its power.

After a few seconds of silence, he ducked into an empty alleyway and turned back into the stark-featured Gehrman Sparrow.

Klein returned to the inn, tapped on the counter, and said Loenese in a Backlund accent, "A room."

The boss looked up and immediately put down what he was holding. He then stood and nodded with a smile.

"Okay, okay.

"Do you need a room with a view of the sea, or something quieter?"

He switched to clumsy Loenese with a thick accent reminiscent of the land of spices, making no further mention of identification.

This really is a pragmatic world... Klein silently lampooned and politely responded, "Quiet."

"Yes, yes, immediately," the boss responded hurriedly.

Then he called an attendant, took the keys, and led Klein personally to the second floor.

"Sir, how many days will you be staying? It's 1 soli 5 pence a night."

"Just tonight." Unable to stand the enthusiasm, Klein answered succinctly.

At the Wind of Azure Inn, his luxurious suite with Danitz was five soli a night.

Without a doubt, the room the boss had chosen was clean and tidy, and there was no trace of the usual humidity in a harbor inn. Klein looked around and nodded in satisfaction.

"Excellent."

"It's my honor," the boss said obsequiously, his fear obvious.

Klein put down his luggage and rested for a while before returning to the ground floor for dinner.

Beside the counter, tables were arranged messily on the first floor. There was a greasy layer on their surface, and a fireplace was burning in the corner, giving off light and heat.

The Rorsted Archipelago lay somewhat to the south, and the lowest temperature in winter was only about 10 degrees Celsius. However, for the locals, this was still cold enough to require a fire for warmth.

Klein randomly found a seat and sat down, ordering a local specialty of grilled meat and spiced mushroom soup, with the main dish being potato bread.

While he was waiting, he swept his gaze over the customers inside the restaurant. His gaze then instinctively landed on a lady.

This lady's black hair was simply tied up, and she had a pair of distinctive greenish-gray eyes. Hers was the sort of appearance one wouldn't easily tire of; the longer he looked, the more intrigued he became.

She was obviously not a native, but she was wearing a man's shirt and a thick tan jacket. By her hand was a round-brimmed hat with a depression in the middle.

This was one of the more common adventurer outfits at sea. The other three men at her table were dressed the same way, and they all clearly appeared weather-beaten.

Klein had never hidden his appreciation of beautiful ladies, but it wasn't her appearance that drew his attention.

The sea was profoundly hostile to women. Those who managed to attain a certain status among adventurers and pirates were either very cunning, very powerful, or both. They were people one had to treat with caution!

Their boots have some fresh mud on them... Did they just come back from the forest? Heh heh, they really are adventurers... Klein made a preliminary judgment based on some clues.

If these four adventurers had come by ferry from Bayam, any sewage or mud they had stepped in would already have dried. Furthermore, as it hadn't rained in town for the past two days, the roads were generally clean and only slightly dusty. With those possibilities eliminated, the only explanation was that they had returned from a trip to the woods outside town.

Klein had heard that many adventurers ventured deep into the primeval forests of the colonial islands in search of abandoned, forgotten pagan temples or altars. Such sites often contained gold and jewels once offered in worship, only to be buried for various reasons in places no one knew. The islands' bars had no shortage of legends about people making fortunes overnight on expeditions into the woods.

There might be evil spirits lingering in those places... It would be better to hunt pirates, or for one to at least acquire the relevant information in advance... Klein retracted his eyes and concentrated on waiting for the food.

The seven Churches classified the various gods believed by the colony's primitive faiths as evil spirits, but Klein believed that some of them were natural spirits.

After a while, the specialty grilled meat was served. It had been cut into small pieces and threaded onto a wooden skewer, then coated with a reddish-brown sauce. Its aroma was rich and its color enticing.

It looks a little like the kebabs from my previous life... In Loen, they usually roast huge chunks of meat and slice them only after they're done... This method lets the flavors penetrate the meat better... Klein picked up the wooden skewer and bit into a piece. Its juices burst into his mouth, and beneath the savory aroma was a hint of sweetness.

It's my type! He nodded in satisfaction.

Klein enjoyed the meal and even tried the local "Gurney Sap," a specialty beverage resembling lemonade with sugar and milk.

He went back to his room. Because he had been hunting the night before, he hadn't slept at all. He washed up early, put out the fireplace, and got into bed. Sleeping too early meant one problem--he would wake up in the middle of the night to pee.

Klein's dream was interrupted. He opened his eyes and slowly accumulated the courage to lift his quilt.

In the middle of the night, Symeem was about 8 to 9 degrees Celsius, sufficiently cold to make itself felt.

After lying still for a while, Klein stretched out his arm and silently withdrew it.

He contemplated for a few seconds before reaching out his hand again and picking up the Sun Brooch from his bedside table.

Although it only produced the spiritual sensation of a hot summer rather than any actual heat, he could at least fool himself into thinking it wasn't cold.

Klein got out of bed and headed for the washroom.

He narrowed his eyes as he relieved the pressure in his lower abdomen.

When he was done, he pulled up his pants and was about to wash his hands when his spiritual perception was triggered.

Klein frowned slightly and looked up at the vent in the bathroom.

Suddenly, something black and slippery dropped and hung there.

It was a venomous snake extending its forked tongue!

Klein was startled. He opened his mouth and shouted, "Bang!"

The snake was struck and split in half.

What happened? Klein stared at it for a few seconds. Seeing no further movement, he walked out of the bathroom and pulled a gold coin from his pocket.

Chapter 533: Mr. 4,200 Pounds

"There is danger in the inn."

Klein softly chanted the divination statement, entered Cogitation, and then flipped a gold coin.

Clang!

A faint but crisp sound echoed in the quiet room. The gold coin tumbled and fell, landing in Klein's palm.

It was tails, indicating a negative response.

No danger... With a frown, Klein looked around and pulled out a bottle of insect-repelling essential oil from his coat on the coat rack.

He sprinkled it in a circle around himself, then quickly took four steps counterclockwise and entered the gray fog to confirm the result through divination.

In about ten seconds, he received a revelation: there was still no danger.

Returning to the real world, Klein shook his head, put the gold coin and bottle away, and donned his coat, trousers, and boots. Then he sat on the edge of the bed and leaned back against the pillow, remaining highly vigilant about this turn of events.

He still remembered his Seer principles, especially the one about "divination is not all-powerful," so even if the answers he received indicated it was safe, he didn't dare to relax and return to his slumber.

This wasn't because he doubted the gray fog's ability to shield him from interference, but because there were too many possibilities. His divination statement might not have been precise enough to cover them all, causing him to misinterpret the result.

He already had a theory about what had just happened. The four adventurers from the restaurant had indeed found an abandoned and forgotten ancient temple in Symeem Island's primeval forest. They had obtained wealth or relics, but they had also disturbed the barely surviving evil spirits and become plagued by evil thoughts and curses.

As time ticked by, Klein suddenly felt a subtle fluctuation of spirituality spread from the room and quickly blend into the surrounding darkness.

The hidden flow that he had barely sensed vanished, and the tranquility of the night returned.

As expected... It's a result of Beyonder powers... It was solved just like that? I'll just wait a little longer. It would be dawn in an hour or two anyway... Even if there really is a problem, this place has a cathedral and a Mandated Punishers team... Klein half closed his eyes and began to engage in Cogitation.

Bayam, Amyris Leaf Bar.

Danitz, determined to deceive anyone monitoring him, held a glass of Lanti Proof and drunkenly watched the girls in the boxing ring perform a sultry dance.

"Dogshit! It's been so long, and they haven't lost a single piece of clothing!" he shouted along with the other drunks.

Then, ignoring whether they received any response, they laughed out loud, clinked glasses, and drank.

"Have you read the newspapers?" a man carousing in the bar late at night asked his companions after a burp.

"Did a donkey eat your brain? Do you think... Do you think I can read those twisting letters of the alphabet? Holy Lord of Storms, I'm only interested in this kind of twisting and turning! Hahaha!" His companion raised his glass and pointed at the dancers, laughing at what he considered a witty answer.

The man who had spoken earlier smacked the back of his head.

"Steel is dead!

"He was killed by Blazing!"

The nearby Danitz was stunned for a moment, then he sat up, turning his head slightly.

He cleared his throat and took a sip of the liquor, acting as if nothing had happened, but he secretly inched closer to hear what the others had to say about the matter.

The adventurer and part-time pirate who had brought him into the trade had once said that life at sea would've been meaningless without liquor, women, and boasting.

"Steel? What Steel? Let me tell you, when I was young, I once defeated a steel pipe!" another drunkard interrupted.

"What did you do to the steel pipe? Should I say something like, whoa, yours sure is thin!" The first man to speak gave a knowing smile.

He didn't wait for a reply as he continued, "Steel Maveti is dead! The second mate of Admiral of Blood is dead!"

The last half of his words emerged as a low growl. It frightened one fellow, already swaying on his feet, into sliding under the table and babbling in terror, "It wasn't me. I didn't do it..."

Dogshit! A bar is always so chaotic! Hurry up and get to the point! I'm waiting for you to praise Lord Blazing! The disguised Danitz couldn't wait to smash the cup of alcohol in his hand at the group.

"The papers say the Navy and the Church did it together. Steel is dead, Blood Brambles is dead, and even Calm Squall is dead. John Smith and that bunch of assholes have been caught as well!" A drunk who was still somewhat lucid came over and joined the conversation.

"No, no, no! That's not the truth!" The man who had first raised the subject shook his head with a smile. "I have a friend at the news agency. He told me they have reliable information that can't be published. The Navy and the Church were merely used. The real killers were Blazing Danitz and a mysterious, experienced, and powerful adventurer, a bounty hunter."

"Impossible! There's absolutely no way Blazing Danitz can defeat Steel! Even if it's a sneak attack, there's no way he can finish Steel!" A few drinkers shared similar viewpoints.

"The key is that powerful adventurer. I suspect he's close to the level of a pirate admiral!" the man who had started the conversation emphasized. "I've no idea how Danitz got to know that guy. Heh, Danitz played a part too. Apparently, he finished off Blood Brambles! Didn't you notice? Blazing's bounty has increased to 4,200 pounds!"

"That's right!"

"Really?"

"Blazing sure isn't simple!"

"What a fearsome pirate, no, a great pirate!"

"Great pirates? I really couldn't tell the last time I drank with him!"

Pui! You son of a bitch, when did I ever drink with you? I don't even know you! Amidst the exclamations, Danitz thought in delight.

4,200 pounds! If those fellows were to know, they would definitely get up at night to wipe the deck in envy. Hahaha, I can now be said to be the strongest boatswain on the Golden Dream!

At that moment, Danitz wished he could fly back to the ship to drink and boast with fellows like Iron Skin and Barrel, telling them all about the thrilling events in Bansy Harbor: how he had defeated the fallen bishop, cleverly escaped Steel and the others' ambush, set a trap to finish off all his enemies, and would never again be able to play the card game of life.

Unfortunately, for Captain's sake, I still have to remain the servant of that madman Gehrman Sparrow... Sigh, I'm already a great 4,200-pound pirate! Danitz sighed and allowed the alcohol to continue numbing him.

As the Storm cathedral's bells rang, the orange sun rose in the sky. The surroundings were still so quiet that only the sound of water could be heard.

Having encountered nothing abnormal, Klein exhaled in relief, took off his clothes, returned to bed, and made up for his lack of sleep with another two hours of rest.

His considerable spirituality allowed him to wake up at the scheduled time of 8:30 a.m. He washed up slowly, changed his clothes, and went to the first floor to buy a loaf of bread with cooked chestnuts and a glass of Gurney Sap. He ate as he walked to the dock.

Since he had already bought his return ticket for the nine o'clock liner, Klein kept an eye on the time and prepared to board.

At that moment, he saw the lady with the greenish-gray eyes, as well as the three male adventurers from her team.

They were buying tickets at the ticket office.

The liner is about to leave. They definitely won't make the nine o'clock departure... I wonder what they obtained from the abandoned temple, but last night they attracted an existence resembling an evil spirit. Please don't let anything unexpected happen when they board the liner... Klein used his body to block the adventurers' line of sight, took out a gold coin, and performed a divination.

His revelation was that the later liner wouldn't be in danger.

Klein hesitated for two seconds, looked at the passengers waiting for the ten o'clock liner near the ticket office, rubbed his fingers together in silence, and lit the ticket in his palm.

He went back to the ticket office as if nothing had happened and bought the ten o'clock liner ticket for four soli.

Then, he went to the washroom at the docks, entered the space above the gray fog, and quickly performed a divination regarding his concerns.

He knew very well that if the results showed that there were great dangers, he wouldn't take the risk of boarding the ship. Instead, he would find a way to prevent the ship from sailing and destroy it, if necessary, to prevent any innocents from dying.

If the revelation was the same as the divination from before, he would ride it normally while keeping a lookout for any accidents.

In the end, he still received a negative answer.

Klein was relieved and returned to the real world. He adjusted the position of the murloc's bladder for quick access.

If an accident really happened, this Beyonder ingredient would become quite important in the vast ocean.

This Beyonder ingredient could also be used in a rudimentary manner, just like the All-Black Eye contaminated by the True Creator.

Ten o'clock sharp.

Klein, in his Gehrman Sparrow appearance, picked up his suitcase, followed the adventurers, and boarded the liner.

Along the way, he appeared to doze or read old newspapers and magazines aboard the ship, but in fact, he was keeping an eye on the greenish-gray-eyed lady and her companions.

This wariness continued all the way to the port of Bayam, but no accidents occurred in the middle.

Did they resolve all their problems? What did they actually obtain? Klein stopped by the roadside, bought the latest newspaper from a newsboy, and watched the four adventurers from the corner of his eye until they disappeared.

Phew... Klein decided not to think about it anymore. As long as it didn't endanger the overall situation, he wasn't concerned about what those people had done. He was merely curious.

He picked up his suitcase, leafing through the papers as he walked down Acid Lemon Street like an ordinary passerby.

Suddenly, with a chuckle, he said to himself, The bounty for Danitz has risen to 4,200 pounds...

If things continued like this, he doubted he could resist the urge to send the fellow to the governor-general's office.

Upon returning to the Wind of Azure Inn, Klein heard undulating snores slowly building to a crescendo before he even took out his keys.

He didn't escape? Klein was slightly surprised, but he wasn't too surprised either.

He had previously tampered with the Shadow Cloak, so if Danitz left, he could use divination to track him down and find Vice Admiral Iceberg.

Although he's timid, he's still cautious... Klein opened the door and entered, looking at Danitz, who awoke with a start. He grinned a little.

"Congratulations, Mr. 4,200 Pounds."

Danitz was instantly wide awake, wanting to laugh dryly in response, but he couldn't.

At this moment, he felt that his life might be in danger.

Chapter 534: A Dream Lesson

Danitz's worries didn't come true. Klein gave him a look and went into the bedroom to rest.

During the five hours he spent on the liner, he had remained highly tense. He had also been startled the previous night and hadn't slept well as a result. Now, he couldn't help but feel exhausted.

With a thud, Klein closed the bedroom door.

Phew... He scared me to death! Danitz relaxed and slumped into the reclining chair.

The image of himself turning into gold pounds had surfaced in his mind a moment ago. He found it difficult to stop such scenes from appearing one after another.

After a moment of silence, Danitz, who had lingered at the bar until dawn, fell asleep again without realizing it. He dreamed that Captain had arrived to save him but failed. Instead, Gehrman Sparrow captured her, and she ended up becoming the crazy adventurer's maidservant.

Just as Danitz, outraged and powerless to resist, was about to wake, the blurry scene around him suddenly became clear. It settled into the luxurious suite at the Wind of Azure Inn.

Knock! Knock! Knock!

Danitz heard a slow, soothing knock on the door.

Wasn't I dreaming? With this question in mind, Danitz walked over to the door and turned the handle.

As the crack in the door widened, he saw a familiar figure.

She was a beautiful lady with an oval face, a high nose bridge, thin lips, and a pair of pale blue eyes that resembled clear spring water.

Her long brown hair was parted in the middle and tied in a simple yet delicate knot at the back of her head, with the rest flowing freely.

She wore no hat, only a beige coat fitted closely at the waist. Her collar was covered with palm-sized flowers woven from white lace.

Her coat was paired with a dark skirt that reached her knees. Its continuous pleats gave it a slightly flared appearance. On her feet was a pair of leather boots the same color as her hair.

"Captain!" Danitz cried out in alarm.

He hurriedly snapped out of his reverie, turned around and faced Gehrman Sparrow's bedroom while taking a defensive stance.

"Be careful! Run! A lunatic is looking for you! He has a terrifying organization backing him!"

As the feeling of self-sacrifice welled up in him, Danitz heard his captain calmly say, "This is a dream."

Dream... Yeah, I'm dreaming, what's there to be afraid of... Danitz glanced left and right, folded his arms, turned around, and said, "Captain, you simulated the powers of a Nightmare? That can't be right, you were near Sonia Island just last week."

Sonia Island was the largest island in this region of the Sonia Sea and the origin of its name. It was almost a small continent. Originally, it was the only remaining gathering place for the elves after the Cataclysm, but as time passed, this ancient Beyonder race was beset by all manner of difficulties and gradually died out. Occasional sightings proved they hadn't gone completely extinct.

At the end of the Fourth Epoch, the Loen Kingdom occupied the island, but it suffered a terrible defeat in the Twenty Year War and ceded Sonia Island to the Feysac Empire. That was already more than seven centuries ago.

Sonia Island was situated north-northwest of the Rorsted Archipelago, and it took nearly half a month by ship to arrive there. Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina Edwards was still in the vicinity of Sonia Island last week, so it was impossible to reach Bayam within a week. Unless she could fly or use the spirit world to travel.

The beautiful lady whom Danitz addressed as Captain nodded.

"We have just entered the Rorsted seas, still a thousand nautical miles away from Bayam."

In other words, she'll take another three or four days to arrive? Now that's normal... Danitz asked curiously, "Shouldn't this already be beyond a Nightmare's range?"

Moreover, it far exceeds it... he added inwardly.

Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina entered the room and walked towards the table and chair.

"It's not a Nightmare power, but a secret ritualistic magic. It uses an item left behind on the ship to enter the dream of someone very far away..."

Listening to Captain's meticulous explanation, Danitz instantly felt as if he had returned to the Golden Dream for the start of another lesson.

I've never heard of such ritualistic magic before... Yes, Captain has mastered many strange and rare forms of magic and witchcraft. No one can tell how much knowledge she possesses... She seems to have mentioned that her Sequence name is Mysticism Magister... Sigh, if I had known she had this "secret technique," I wouldn't have been so frustrated over how to inform her about the unexpected events in Bayam... A flurry of thoughts distracted Danitz from Edwina's description.

"Captain, you sensed that there's a problem with our point of contact over here?"

"Yes, this is another secret technique..." Edwina seemed inclined to describe the specific method in detail.

Seeing this, Danitz hurriedly sighed.

"Poor Old Rinn and the others..."

Edwina stopped walking and turned her back to the window, asking succinctly, "What happened?"

"I'll have to start from Damir Harbor." Danitz felt energized, as if his long-lasting depression had finally paid off at that moment.

He described his attempt to recruit Gehrman Sparrow, how he had ended up discovering that the man was a lunatic, and his own, somewhat exaggerated, plight aboard the White Agate.

Following the rough account he had prepared the previous night, he thoroughly described the bizarre and horrifying events at Bansy Harbor, including Steel Maveti's ambush and assassination attempt. He explained how he had joined forces with Gehrman Sparrow to counterattack and hunt their enemies, as well as his own theories about Gehrman Sparrow's background and powers. These included Creeping Hunger and the powerful, secretive organization.

Throughout the account, he tried his best to recount the truth, exaggerating only the role he had played and elevating his status from servant or attendant to assistant or collaborator.

Vice Admiral Edwina listened quietly the entire time without interrupting him. When he was done, she nodded lightly and said, "He has no ill intentions."

Him? Gehrman Sparrow means no harm? Danitz quickly said, "Captain, regardless, he's a dangerous fellow!

"Are you sure he means no harm?"

"I can't be certain," Edwina replied calmly.

"Then why did you..." Danitz took a silent breath, sensing the similarity between his captain and Gehrman Sparrow. Neither was someone he could converse with normally.

Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina said with a straight face, "That's just my speculation and judgment."

"..."

Danitz rubbed his temples with his right hand. "Anyway, he's dangerous. There's a secret organization backing him, and I've no idea about the situation of the organization. Captain, I don't think you should take the risk of communicating with him, even if he claims to have something to ask of you."

Edwina thought for a moment before saying, "There's no need to take risks.

"I can communicate with him through you."

Danitz first heaved a sigh of relief before asking both curiously and expectantly, "Captain, how will that work? Or should I say, what do I need to do?"

Edwina raised her right hand, causing a stand and blackboard to appear in the dream.

"I need you to hold a ritual," she said.

"It's called a Soulfall Ritual. It allows my soul to pass through the spirit world and attach itself to your body. I'll be able to converse directly with Gehrman Sparrow. It works through anyone below the demigod level, within an effective range of no more than 500 nautical miles...

"It involves reason and communication, and it belongs to the realm of the God of Knowledge and Wisdom. You must draw the corresponding symbols and magic labels...

"In mysticism, the Blue Planet corresponds to the God of Knowledge and Wisdom. Mercury, brass, lavender, pepper, and mint are needed...

"The Blue Planet corresponds to Saturday. The time of the Blue Planet is from midnight to one on Friday, and eleven to twelve noon on Saturday..."

Edwina explained as she wrote the key points to remember on the blackboard, and Danitz instinctively sat down and assumed a listening posture.

As he listened, he suddenly felt at a loss.

Why do I need to attend classes even in my dreams?

In the bedroom, Klein, who was sleeping, suddenly jolted awake, having vaguely sensed something.

He got out of bed and listened for a moment. In the living room, although Danitz was snoring, his breathing was calmer than before.

It wasn't particularly strange, but it was unusual enough to alert an experienced mysticism expert like Klein, who had been through a great deal.

He softly walked to the door that separated the bedroom from the living room, reached for the handle, and slowly turned it.

Without a sound, Klein walked out of the bedroom and saw Danitz lying in a reclining chair, fast asleep. Everything around him appeared normal.

Klein quietly activated his Spirit Vision and examined Blazing, but found nothing amiss. Both the color of his aura and the changes in his mood were normal and within a reasonable range.

After watching for a while, Klein frowned and took out a silver charm.

"Dream Charm!"

In the dream, Danitz was learning the Soulfall Ritual with a bitter expression. He had no doubt that the captain in front of him was genuine.

No one can fake such a style and hobby!

At that moment, he heard the sound of the lock turning.

Subconsciously, Danitz looked towards the bedroom, where the crack in the door was widening, and Gehrman Sparrow, who was wearing only a white shirt, walked out expressionlessly.

"You! How did you appear here!" Danitz shot to his feet, blurting out in shock.

He quickly snapped to his senses and stammered, "Th-this is my dream!"

How did Gehrman Sparrow get in here so easily?!

With one hand in the pocket of his dark trousers, Klein walked toward the woman who had her back to the window and said in a low voice, "A charm."

Then, looking directly at the woman, he asked almost with certainty, "Edwina Edwards?"

Her attire is a little strange... She doesn't look like an adventurer, much less a pirate. She looks like a woman with a respectable job, someone capable of supporting herself... Her clothing appears to be in the Intis style... Klein thought, finding the sight somewhat unfamiliar.

Edwina nodded slightly and similarly replied with a question, "Gehrman Sparrow?"

"Yes, good afternoon, Ma'am." Klein smiled slightly, pressed his hand to his chest, and bowed.

Edwina nodded and replied, "Good afternoon."

Klein, who was maintaining his persona, stopped talking, waiting for the other side to take the initiative to ask him about his purpose.

He looked at Edwina.

Edwina looked at him.

There was a sudden silence in the dream for several minutes.

From time to time, Danitz looked to the right and to the left, having a baffling feeling that perhaps all of this might really be a dream.

Chapter 535: Fond of Teaching

Unable to tolerate it any longer, Danitz decided to do something.

He coughed twice, faced Vice Admiral Iceberg Edwina Edwards, and said, "Captain, Mr. Gehrman Sparrow has some questions for you."

Phew... Klein let out a sigh of relief.

If this staring contest continued, he felt as though his persona would collapse.

Edwina nodded gently, looked at Klein, and said, "Mr. Sparrow, have you received an education?"

What kind of question is this? Klein's expression didn't change as he indifferently replied, "University graduate."

Ah? Danitz looked at Gehrman Sparrow in surprise, unable to believe the fact that this crazy adventurer and bounty hunter had actually gone to university and even graduated!

He had no way of reconciling a university, a hallmark of wisdom, with Gehrman Sparrow!

Unless it's some "Hunter University" or "Killer Academy"... Or a university that exists only in the fantasies of madmen and psychopaths... Danitz couldn't help but lampoon silently.

Edwina didn't seem surprised. She continued to ask, "What languages have you mastered?"

"..."

Klein resisted the urge to frown and said, "Jotun, Elvish, Dragonese, ancient Hermes, Hermes, ancient Feysac, Loenese..."

Edwina responded tersely.

"Do you understand the other domains in mysticism?"

For a moment, Klein felt as though he had returned to his elementary school days in his previous life. Because he had enrolled late, a teacher had asked him a series of questions: how many Chinese characters he knew, whether he had mastered the four basic arithmetic operations, whether he knew the English alphabet, and how many Tang dynasty poems he could recite...

Calm down... You're Gehrman Sparrow now... Klein once again said without emotion, "Adept."

Edwina thought for two seconds before explaining unhurriedly, "I need to tailor my answers to your level of knowledge so that you can understand them more easily."

This... Klein was surprised to find that the beautiful lady before him was nothing like an adventurer who engaged in piracy on the side. She was essentially a teacher with a cold expression but a kind and patient manner.

He calmed down after thinking about the vampire who headed to the Church of Earth Mother every day.

This is the first pirate admiral I've formally met. She's indeed unique... Klein sighed inwardly and said, "First question: where do the Beyonder creatures commonly encountered at sea appear?"

He didn't ask about mermaids directly, as he didn't want her to see through his true purpose and cause unnecessary complications.

Edwina thought for a moment and then wiped the "Soulfall Ritual" off the blackboard. She then wrote and drew:

"Set off from Sonia Island and sail 1,200 nautical miles in this direction, and you'll enter depths of the sea that humans have never explored.

"A Naga tribe is active there. They've been working hard to find an underwater city rumored to have a powerful demon sealed within. That demon is the object of their faith..."

Edwina spoke in detail, drawing partly on her own efforts to find the treasure and partly on knowledge and rumors she had gathered through exchanges with others.

It took her a while to finish the general account, but to Klein's dismay, there were no mermaids anywhere except in the eastern waters of the Gargas Archipelago.

He quickly calmed his mood and said, "Second question, do you know how to remove the mental corruption from a Beyonder characteristic?"

He didn't hide the fact that he knew about Beyonder characteristics.

Edwina's eyes flickered for the first time, as though she had a new understanding of Gehrman Sparrow.

She shook her head.

"I don't know.

"However, I once imagined that it might be possible to borrow an external force and completely crush the corrupted Beyonder characteristic, reducing it to the smallest specks of light. This way, whether it is corruption or a curse, it will scatter upon losing its vessel, then vanish rapidly or slowly. As for the shattered Beyonder characteristic, its particular traits will cause it to gather again bit by bit and eventually take form once more.

"Unfortunately, only gods can do this. If you gain the favor of any god, you can try to please 'Them' by offering enough sacrifices and praying for a response."

The law of Beyonder characteristic convergence... Klein mentally supplied what Edwina had deliberately left unsaid.

At the same time, he couldn't help but sigh. Vice Admiral Iceberg, Captain of the Golden Dream, was extremely knowledgeable in mysticism. She was no less knowledgeable than he was, despite his having read many of Roselle's diary entries and experienced so much.

It was said that Vice Admiral Iceberg could replicate or simulate her opponents' Beyonder powers when they used them in front of her... This is rather similar to Mr. Isengard Stanton's ring, 2-081... As soon as Klein's thoughts began to wander, he forcefully reined them in and nodded as though considering something.

"I agree with this hypothesis."

"Regrettably, I haven't tested it in practice. It's only a conjecture." Edwina rarely used words that expressed emotion.

Danitz, who had been listening by the side, had a dazed look on his face. He was unable to keep up with the conversation at all.

What are they talking about? What are they discussing? Why do I know every word, yet understand nothing when they're strung together... What's a Beyonder characteristic? What happens when it's shattered? What does it mean for it to gather again? Danitz looked from side to side, his eyes blank.

At this point, Klein threw out the third question he had thought about.

"Where can you find ancient wraiths?"

He only mentioned this kind of monster because the corresponding ingredients could also be used in the domain of Death. It didn't expose the secrets of his own Sequence.

Edwina glanced at Danitz and said, "I'll keep an eye out for you.

"If you want to attend Bayam's Beyonder gatherings, he knows a few. He can take you there."

Not bad, it's easier to talk to smart people... Klein added, "Do you know where there are people who can make mystical items out of Beyonder ingredients or characteristics?"

"I met one at a gathering, but he never appeared again," Edwina answered frankly. "I've mastered the relevant knowledge and can replicate the process, but I cannot guarantee its success rate."

Heh heh... I'll wait then... Klein didn't harp on the topic and switched to asking, "How much do you know about Death's Key?"

"Little." An iron-black key suddenly appeared in Edwina's hand. It was about the size of a lyre, ancient in shape, and possessed a dark luster.

Holding up the item from the shipwreck, the pirate admiral said, "If you mean this key, then I can tell you that it belonged to the giants."

Klein glanced sideways at Danitz.

"Your boatswain told me that it can also belong to a dragon or a demonic wolf."

"That was our initial guess. I've recently found some historical documents, and with the help of the other items on the ship, I've formed a preliminary conclusion." Edwina showed no agitation. She remained patient, though her expression was cold.

After finally regaining his sense as the owner of the dream, Danitz curiously asked, "Captain, is it really the giants'?"

"Yes." Edwina gave an affirmative answer. "That batch of documents showed that there were still many giants active in the Fourth Epoch. Some of them had switched faiths to the God of Combat, while the others were left scattered, becoming the hunting targets of the humans.

"One of the tribes built a ship and set out to find the lost Giant King's Court at sea, but never returned. The sunken ship we discovered, along with some of the items inside, closely resembles the relics they left behind in their original settlement. Therefore, we have sufficient reason to believe that the key belonged to the giants and very likely points to their race's lost Giant King's Court from before the Cataclysm."

Giant King's Court? Little Sun said it's not far from the City of Silver... Klein didn't say a word, but he quietly listened to Vice Admiral Iceberg's description.

I have to say, my knowledge of the City of Silver and the Forsaken Land of the Gods far surpasses that of other Beyonders... Hmm, even the seven gods can't find that place, or at least have difficulty determining its exact situation. In this respect, I'm better informed than 'Them'... Klein reflected with a mixture of smugness and self-deprecation before asking in a deep voice, "If I offer a sufficient price, would you be willing to sell the key?"

"Of course, unless I acquire additional clues to the Giant King's Court." Edwina continued to speak at a moderate pace.

Very good, if the Tarot Club organizes an expedition to the Giant King's Court one day, I will buy this key... Klein was about to ask the last question when he suddenly remembered the four adventurers.

Relying on the power of the Dream Charm, he conjured the lady with greenish-gray eyes, who wore a man's shirt, and her three companions.

"Do you know them?"

Edwina glanced at them and said almost without thinking, "Leticia Dolera, an archaeologist and adventurer.

"Rumor has it that she's a member of the Moses Ascetic Order or the Element Dawn."

Moses Ascetic Order... The ancient organization that believes in the Hidden Sage... It really isn't for money, and their goal is definitely something from the abandoned temple... Klein asked casually, "What do you know about Element Dawn?"

This mysterious organization was similar to the Psychology Alchemists, having arisen within the past century or two. It combined elements from various schools of mysticism and had its own theoretical system. Its members were all proficient in spellcasting and had mastered many ancient witchcraft techniques.

Edwina pursed her lips and said, "They've always been fighting against the Moses Ascetic Order.

"Rumor has it that the eldest daughter of Emperor Roselle, Bernadette Gustav, established it."

The Element Dawn is headed by Bernadette? Zaratul's prophecy is quite accurate after all. She can really be considered an important figure in the Beyonder world... Klein sighed inwardly, feeling somewhat enlightened.

He resisted the urge to sigh and looked at Edwina. He calmly said, "One last question."

When the beautiful lady and Blazing Danitz had focused their full attention on him, he slowly opened his mouth.

"Are you interested in cooperating?"
