"What kind of cooperation?" Edwina asked in return.

The smile on Klein's face grew ever more exuberant, with a hint of madness in it, as he said, "Hunt Admiral of Blood."

Chapter 536: Local Faith

What? Hunt Admiral of Blood? Danitz almost raised his right hand to his ear.

His first reaction was that he had misheard.

However, the madness concealed in Gehrman Sparrow's smile and the slight twitch of Captain's eyebrows, an unusual reaction from her, confirmed that he had heard correctly!

This... This madman Gehrman Sparrow really is daring! This is one of the seven Pirate Admirals. They're nothing like someone of Steel Maveti's caliber! Danitz's pupils shrank as his heart roared.

Putting aside Admiral of Blood Senor's own might, the pirates under him alone are intimidating enough. His first mate, third mate, and the captain of each ship are all capable of independently leading a large pirate crew!

And the men Steel Maveti had brought to Bayam were only a fraction of the Blood pirate crew!

When Vice Admiral Hurricane Qilangos died silently like a wild dog in Backlund, it was mainly because he wasn't with his pirate crew. However, Admiral of Blood Senor is different. He seldom leaves his flagship and rarely leaves the company of his men.

Even considering Senor's strength alone, he would still be extremely terrifying. He's mysterious and bizarre, one of the few powerhouses at sea second only to the Four Kings. Even Captain might not dare claim to be his superior... A madman's courage really does exceed my imagination. No, he doesn't know the meaning of fear at all! The death and replacement of a pirate admiral would definitely send shock waves through the Five Seas! One thought after another flashed through Danitz's mind, but in the end, he strangely calmed down.

It was because he recalled that Gehrman Sparrow was a powerhouse at the level of the seven admirals, and there might be a hidden, terrifying organization backing him.

Edwina said after a few seconds of silence, "Do you know of the Rose School of Thought?"

Not only do I know them, I've also killed their people and taken their mystical items... Wait a minute, why do I have to describe myself as a bad guy... Klein restrained his smile and said unperturbedly, "I've hunted their members."

Edwina fell silent again. Beside her, Danitz's thoughts began racing once more: What are they talking about? What's the Rose School of Thought? Where am I? Whose dream is this?

After a short silence, Edwina said, "After the huge battle with Vice Admiral Dusk's pirate crew, Senor's crew vanished. At present, no one knows where they are. We will have to wait patiently."

Is this an agreement? Klein once again revealed a smile.

"I do not lack patience.

"How can I contact you?"

Edwina turned her head to look at Danitz.

"He knows how."

Me? Soulfall Ritual? Wait a minute, Captain, you mean I have to follow this madman, Gehrman Sparrow, during this period of time? No! Who knows when this fellow will go crazy! With a start, Danitz quickly opened his mouth and said, "Captain, I've already left the Golden Dream for too long. I've missed too many lessons! I can't wait to return!"

He tried his hardest to show his sincerity in his eyes, filling them with the desire for knowledge.

"I believe we can switch to someone else, like Iron Skin and Barrel..."

Before he could finish, Edwina suddenly raised her right hand and pressed it to her ear.

She looked slightly to the side and said without expression, "What did you say?

"I couldn't hear it.

"Roselle's Dream Spell is reaching its limits..."

The pirate admiral's skirt fluttered slightly as she stepped back. Her figure quickly faded, dissolving into motes of light.

The rest of Danitz's words remained stuck in his throat. He stretched out his right hand in an attempt to grasp something, but it fell weakly to his side.

Roselle's Dream Spell? The Emperor's presence in the field of mysticism is also quite formidable... The full name of the Vice Admiral Iceberg is Edwina Edwards... Edwards. Isn't this the surname of one of the emperor's Four Horsemen of the Apocalypse? A descendant? However, from the tone of Vice Admiral Iceberg, she isn't familiar with the eldest daughter of the emperor, Bernadette. They might not even know each other... Klein looked at Danitz, whose expression had completely collapsed, and let out a soft laugh.

"Perhaps one day I will call you Mr. 10,000 Pounds."

"..."

Danitz straightened his back in fright, then he saw Gehrman Sparrow's figure disappear from his dream.

If I'm only Sequence 7 and have a bounty of seven or eight thousand pounds, I'll be targeted everywhere I go... Danitz stood rooted in his dream, his head aching more the longer he thought about it.

In the evening, Danitz looked at Gehrman Sparrow, who was dressed as a native.

"Today, we'll be visiting the local faction. We might be able to find some good things there. Heh heh, they have a great need for Beyonder ingredients, food, and weapons."

At this point, Danitz looked Gehrman Sparrow up and down. It was rare for him to find something he did better than the other man. He said with a smile, "Their district is populated almost entirely by locals. There aren't even many people of mixed descent. If there's the slightest irregularity in your clothing, you'll be discovered.

"Your pantaloons and brown jacket are fine, but you can't wear that shirt underneath. A real native wears a Taraba shirt. It's similar to a Sea Soul shirt, but entirely blue or white. They don't wear caps, headscarves, or anything like that.

"Also, someone like you who obviously looks Loenese will face severe discrimination and hostility there, like a wolf sneaking into a flock of sheep. You won't be able to blend in at all..."

Before he could finish, he saw Gehrman Sparrow tilt his head and look at him. His sharp facial features quickly softened, and his fair skin instantly turned bronze. In a heartbeat, he had become an unmistakable native who didn't stand out in any way.

"..."

After a few seconds of silence, Danitz laughed dryly as if nothing had happened. "Me too. I have to disguise myself."

Your disguise? Heh... Klein took off his cap and sat down.

"What local faction?"

It actually trades Beyonder ingredients, food, and weapons?

"Ahem, to put it simply... the Resistance." Danitz suddenly wasn't sure whether Gehrman Sparrow had any connections to Loen officials.

Klein was startled for a second.

"Resistance?"

Danitz smiled awkwardly and said, "People who clamor for independence and demand that the natives govern their own affairs. Their encampments are deep in the forests or out at sea. They cooperate with many pirates and adventurers. Of course, their main backers are from Feysac and Intis. Priests of the God of Combat and the Eternal Blazing Sun often come here in secret."

Bayam isn't so peaceful after all... After leaving the kingdom and coming to sea, Klein finally understood what international conflict truly meant. It was nothing like merely reading newspaper reports about the wars in East Balam.

He nodded slightly and didn't object to visiting the local faction.

After leaving the Wind of Azure Inn, Klein followed Danitz, whose face was painted black. They went through the streets, heading all the way southeast.

Leaving the confines of the City of Generosity, they entered a district with a distinctive architectural style. The buildings had elevated floors supported by wooden pilings. Some were three- or four-story structures derived from Loen-style terraced houses.

The roads that split from each other were narrow and dirty, with many stalls on either side. They sold all kinds of headgear, earrings, and accessories made of stone. They were mostly bright red or multicolored.

"What a bunch of strange fellows. They especially like bright colors, just like those venomous snakes in the forest," Danitz whispered.

As someone from Intis, you aren't much better. You revere the color gold and prize luxury and refinement, just like a nouveau riche... Klein lampooned inwardly.

They passed by a group of dark-skinned, skinny, and shriveled natives, entering the alleys which had clothes drying over their heads. The scene before their eyes suddenly opened up, revealing a small municipal square.

In the square, a group of locals had gathered around the central pond. Some knelt, some prostrated themselves, some murmured, and some sang softly. Their expressions were devout, but their eyes were numb.

As soon as they realized that someone was approaching, they quickly got up and ran into the surrounding alleys in a flurry.

All the windows on the second, third, and fourth floors of the surrounding houses clanged shut, and the square grew quiet, but Klein's spiritual perception told him that there were many people behind the windows, around the alleys, in the dark corners, watching these strangers who had suddenly arrived in their world.

Danitz lowered his head and said in a suppressed voice, "Don't worry, this is their way of protecting themselves."

"Oh?" Klein expressed his doubts.

Danitz let out a chuckle.

"Before this place was fully colonized, the natives of the Rorsted Archipelago all believed in Sea God Kalvetua. They believed this deity, who appeared as a gigantic sea serpent, would protect the islands from being devoured by earthquakes or tsunamis.

"Now that this belief has been outlawed, the Church of the Lord of Storms has been constantly fighting against the heretics. Even the Church of Evernight and the Church of Steam and Machinery are unable to expand their influence here, and there are only a few cathedrals.

"But how can a faith that has endured for centuries, if not a millennium, be so easily eradicated? There are still plenty of Sea God believers in Bayam, on Blue Mountain Island, and throughout the Rorsted Sea. Even if a group is captured every month or two and subjected to all manner of brutal punishment, the situation can't be reversed anytime soon. The believers of the Sea God form the main pillar of the Resistance's support.

"In my opinion, it will take at least another hundred years before the faith of the Sea God will be completely eradicated. Of course, that's under the premise that there aren't any other disruptive elements."

Sea God Kalvetua... Its image is of a gigantic sea serpent... As Klein listened thoughtfully, he followed Danitz into a four-story house on the right. He went to the top floor via a narrow staircase.

Knock! Knock! Knock! Danitz knocked on the door to his left.

"Who is it?" someone asked in a low voice.

Danitz replied with a chuckle, "A friend who brings wine and barbecue."

"Where from?" the person inside asked a strange question.

Danitz stepped back.

"At sea."

Creak. The door slowly opened and Klein saw a naked arm.

The arm was tattooed with a blue, hideous sea serpent.

Chapter 537: Excessive Spiritual Perception

Behind the door was a bare-chested man with a hideous blue sea serpent tattooed on his arm. Short streaks of red paint marked his cheeks, chest, and abdomen, each in groups of three.

How exotic... But aren't you afraid of being too conspicuous? Aren't you afraid the police will catch you when you go outside? You're part of the Resistance, which operates in secret! Klein was about to look away when the man's thick, unruly eyebrows and cold-blooded eyes nearly made him frown.

He has killed many people... Klein made a rough judgment based on his spiritual intuition.

In truth, because of his identity and the knowledge from his previous life, he initially felt considerable sympathy for rebels under colonial rule. However, after hearing about the acts this man and Sea God Kalvetua's followers commonly committed, he grew even warier and more repulsed.

This wasn't to say that he discriminated against the local faith. Rather, he understood that the traditional religions of the colonial islands remained at a primitive stage centered on sacrifice. Their worship involved blood and living offerings, and their believers remained steeped in ignorance.

In addition, judging by the Emperor's experiences and my own, this world's Beyonder nature is steeped in madness and corruption. A "deity" still at the primitive stage of demanding sacrifices is essentially unable to resist this tendency. It's quite obvious how such an entity would behave... Without a word, Klein followed Danitz into the room.

"Edmonton, who's here?" A mild voice sounded from the area near the windows.

The tattooed man closed the door and said, "They've disguised themselves."

At that moment, Klein had taken a good look at the situation in the room and gained a basic understanding of it.

The living room wasn't very large. A cupboard, a table, and a few chairs were enough to make it feel cramped.

To the right were two doors leading to what appeared to be bedrooms; to the left was a "kitchen" partitioned off by a cabinet. As for a private washroom, there undoubtedly wasn't one. On his way upstairs, Klein had discovered a communal washroom in the corner of every landing. The stench from their long neglect urged passersby to climb faster.

Directly in front of him was a window. Two bamboo poles hung out and were used to hang a lot of clothes to dry.

Four or five men sat or stood in the bedroom doorway and living room. All were dark-skinned natives with slightly curly hair. They wore dark-blue Taraba shirts and varying amounts of red paint on their exposed skin. As for the sea serpent tattoos, Klein couldn't tell whether they had any beneath their clothes.

Some had revolvers at their waists, while others carried brownish-red hunting rifles. Some even carried gray steel backpacks and long, thick, high-pressure steam rifles. They formed a semicircle around Danitz and Klein, who had just entered the room.

The person who spoke was a wheelchair-bound man in his forties. He wore a jacket and had a blanket over his knees.

He had a shaved head, a slightly green beard on both sides of his face, and his dark brown eyes were calm and collected.

He glanced at his visitors and slowly smiled.

"Blazing."

Danitz was stunned for a moment before forcing out a laugh.

"Kalat, you have a good eye."

Dogshit! Is my disguise really that bad? he roared inwardly, unwilling to accept it.

Kalat ignored Blazing's insincere praise and chuckled.

"I heard that you killed Steel and Blood Brambles?"

"How else would they be dead?" Danitz retorted without hesitation.

Kalat narrowed his eyes, slowly shifting his gaze to Klein, who had a featureless face.

He knew very well that Blazing Danitz alone would have had difficulty killing Blood Brambles Hendry, much less Steel Maveti. Rumor had it that he had succeeded with the help of a powerful adventurer, a seasoned bounty hunter.

Is it the person beside him? When he looked into Klein's eyes, he didn't see any nervousness, worry, or vigilance. Those eyes were like a deep ocean.

It could very well be... At the very least, he's stronger than Blazing! He signaled to Edmonton and the others with his eyes, secretly informing them to be on their guard.

"What are you doing here?" Kalat didn't press the subject.

Danitz subconsciously looked at Klein, and after seeing his nod of affirmation, he replied, "Here to see what good things you have to offer."

Kalat pointed to a table and said, "It's all placed there."

Many strange items were laid out, including a bone whistle, a crude bagpipe, an iron-black leaf, and a bloodstained rock...

Without waiting for Klein and Danitz to examine the items, Kalat clapped his hands and said, "I have a mission.

"If you can complete it, you can freely choose an item from these at no additional cost."

He laughed and added, "By the standards of you foreigners, they aren't mystical items, but each possesses some supernatural power. That power will slowly, yes, slowly weaken until it disappears."

"What mission?" Klein asked calmly, having no intention of hiding the fact that Danitz was only a follower.

He reached into the blanket that extended to his knees and took out a stack of white paper.

"Find out where they are.

"If you can catch them directly, you will get even more."

He raised the papers and began displaying lifelike portraits, including one of a lady with greenish-gray eyes dressed in a man's shirt.

Leticia Dolera... Klein recognized at a glance whom the Resistance was looking for.

It was the female archaeologist and adventurer he had met the previous night and shared a ship with that morning. She was suspected of being a member of the Moses Ascetic Order or the Element Dawn.

Danitz looked at it closely for two seconds and found the picture vaguely familiar.

Suddenly, he remembered where he had seen her before.

Gehrman Sparrow had shown her to him in the dream!

He only asked Captain about her this afternoon, and we've already run into something connected to her this evening... Isn't he a little too good at gathering information? Danitz resisted the urge to glance at the crazy adventurer beside him, afraid that Kalat and Edmonton would notice something amiss.

He was very experienced in this area.

The Resistance, who believe in the Sea God, are looking for Leticia... The faith of the Sea God spreads throughout the Rorsted Archipelago, including Symeem... There was a snake last night... The image of the Sea God is that of a gigantic sea serpent... Klein superimposed the two matters together and quickly came to a preliminary conclusion.

Archaeologist Leticia and her companions had obtained an important item connected to the Sea God from the forgotten temple in Symeem Island's ancient forest. This had prompted both the probing the previous night and the Resistance's search!

Klein thought for a moment, then he gave a perfunctory reply, "I'll keep an eye out."

I won't randomly get myself involved in matters related to evil spirits. Of course, if it's necessary, then I will report it to the officials... he silently added a few words inwardly.

Kalat nodded and said, "Take a look first and see if there's anything you want."

Klein walked over and was just about to make inquiries while examining the selection when he suddenly sensed something and subconsciously looked toward the right side of the pile.

There was a short sword made from a slender bone. It was slightly longer than a forearm and milky white, with several deep, dark-red stripes across it.

It can trigger my spiritual perception... Klein stretched out his right hand to pick up the sharp bone sword at the very front and examine it carefully.

The moment his fingers touched the bone sword, cries of despair and pain echoed in his mind. The heavy stench of blood assailed his nose, and he seemed to see numerous distorted, rotting, illusory figures covered in mucus.

Klein's forehead ached, as though he had been pierced by a needle, and he subconsciously retracted his finger.

A little sinister... It's not a simple item... Klein, who had experienced things far more intense before, only showed a slight change in expression.

He resisted the urge to activate his Spirit Vision, afraid that he would see something he shouldn't.

Noticing this, Kalat exchanged glances with Edmonton and said with a smile, "This bone sword is capable of draining an enemy's blood. It's not bad. Do you want it?"

A little too eager... Klein frowned, immediately smoothed his brow, and said in a deep voice, "No, there's nothing I want here."

If Kalat hadn't asked, he might even have bought the bone sword and studied it above the gray fog. However, the man's eagerness to promote it made him wary. He therefore abandoned his original plan.

Kalat crossed his hands and said, "It's not expensive at all.

"Or would you like to look at something else?"

"There's no need." Klein's pupils shrank suddenly as he turned and walked straight towards the door.

Danitz hesitated momentarily before hurrying after him.

Edmonton, who had a sea serpent tattoo on his arm, watched silently. He seemed as if he would reach out and stop them at any moment, but in the end, he didn't do anything.

They were the powerful adventurers who had killed Steel Maveti and Blood Brambles Hendry!

Once out of the room, Klein stomped down the stairs without saying a word. Danitz ran after him, alarmed.

Trusting his reading of the situation, he asked no questions and simply followed.

The two quickly returned to the square, and the crowd which had gathered once again to either kneel or prostrate scattered once more.

But unlike before, one man remained kneeling motionlessly on the ground.

Klein didn't even glance at him as he walked past without stopping.

Danitz, however, glanced over subconsciously, only to discover that the man's face was as dry as weathered rock.

Pa!

A piece of flesh from the man's cheek fell to the ground; it was a piece of grayish skin with facial hair attached to it.

He seemed to have lost all the moisture in his body.

Danitz was startled and didn't dare to look again, feeling that things had become strange and dangerous at some point.

The two of them passed through the alleys, left the city, and boarded a rental carriage.

The carriage driver was clearly a native, about forty years old, with a very pleasant laugh.

But he didn't say a word along the way. The silence made Danitz's heartbeat sound like a drum.

Klein pursed his lips and remained silent.

The rental carriage quickly arrived at the dock area. In order to change his attire, Danitz got it to stop a distance from Acid Lemon Street.

After getting off the carriage, Klein didn't pay the fare or stop. He immediately took large strides and left, leaving Danitz stunned.

He tossed two soli to the carriage driver and hurriedly chased after Gehrman Sparrow.

After a few steps, he looked back and saw the driver kneeling down with a face full of zeal and devoutness. He leaned over the ground and kissed the ground where Klein had stepped.

Chapter 538: Dispel

What's happening?

Danitz couldn't believe what he was seeing.

The sight wasn't shocking in itself, but it filled him with inexplicable terror. It was like drunkenly plunging into a bucket of ice, a bone-piercing chill spreading from the soles of his feet to the crown of his head.

What exactly happened? Why did things become so strange? Danitz drew a breath, forced himself to turn away, and chased after Gehrman Sparrow.

He found the crazy adventurer walking faster and faster, to the point that he had to jog to keep up.

After crossing the street and passing through an alley, Danitz suddenly saw a greenish shadow fall from a tree.

Pa! Its scaly, slippery body twisted beneath its triangular head as it flicked out a scarlet forked tongue. It was a rather long venomous snake!

Dogshit! Why would there be snakes in this season? Danitz wasn't afraid of such creatures. He had even roasted snake meat before, but it was the abnormality of everything that left him agitated.

Circling around the coiling snake, Danitz subconsciously looked left and right and found that on both sides of the gutter, in the corners of ruined houses, and on the insides of rusty water pipes, there were pairs of cold, vertical, and different-colored eyes looking out.

Danitz felt a chill run down his spine, as if his scalp had been pierced by a dense array of fine needles.

He didn't dare stay any longer, nor did he dare strike out on his own. He increased his speed again and followed closely behind Gehrman Sparrow.

Entering the Wind of Azure Inn, he couldn't help but look up as he walked up the wooden stairs, wanting to ask something.

Suddenly, his chest tightened and his breathing stagnated.

At that moment, it was as though he had dived to the bottom of the sea, with heavy torrents pressing down on him from every direction.

Whoosh!

Faintly, Danitz heard the rush of the tide and saw a faint, illusory light surround Gehrman Sparrow, as if it were forming an endless, bottomless blue ocean.

Within that ocean, there was an enormous azure-blue figure holding everything up. It resembled a tower.

This... Danitz's right foot stopped in mid-air, unable to lower his foot.

He wasn't unfamiliar with such feelings. Back at the last pirate convention, the King of the Five Seas, Nast, was even more imposing and terrifying than this. Almost all the pirates couldn't lift their heads, and even powerhouses at the admiral rank could only barely resist.

Danitz knew very well that it wasn't a result of Gehrman Sparrow's own powers.

If he was at the level of a demigod, there was no need to include the Mandated Punishers during his hunt of Steel Maveti!

Ocean... Tide... These two terms flashed through Danitz's mind, and he immediately thought of the matter of the Resistance's faith in Sea God Kalvetua.

C-could Gehrman Sparrow have been afflicted by the Sea God's curse through some unknown process when he touched the bone sword? Was the devotee's desiccated corpse one of the conditions for activating the curse? Did the carriage driver prostrate himself and kiss the ground because he sensed the Sea God's aura?

Man... Gehrman Sparrow will probably die here today... Should I keep my distance to avoid being implicated? At most... At the very most, I'll come back to collect his corpse...

It might still be possible to save him. I can use the Soulfall Ritual to seek Captain's help. She knows so many strange secret techniques. She should be able to resolve this problem... No, Lord. The Soulfall Ritual only works within 500 nautical miles. They're still en route and won't enter that range for at least half a day...

As he desperately tried to think of a solution, Danitz stopped panicking and followed Gehrman Sparrow steadily into the luxurious suite.

Klein remained silent, but his dark brown eyes seemed tinged with a deep azure-blue that approached black.

He went straight to the bedroom and locked the door behind him.

Danitz stood outside, torn between fleeing and saving the man.

Inside the bedroom, Klein closed his eyes, waiting for the right opportunity.

Suddenly, he took four steps counterclockwise, chanting a statement with each one.

A wave of manic, high-pitched ravings rang in his ears as his Spirit Body shot into the gray fog.

Without a sound, he heard an indescribable, shrill, and painful howl.

Klein appeared within the ancient palace, at the very end of the long, mottled table.

In the motionless gray fog below, an illusory, gigantic azure-blue sea serpent appeared.

It was located in an ancient dark ruin, coiled around a half-collapsed pillar. Its hideous head was raised, and its bloody mouth was wide open, revealing many curved fangs that were even longer than a human's forearm.

Layers of blood and mucus flowed over its milky-white fangs.

The sea serpent thrashed its tail madly, setting off terrifying waves and enormous ripples that made the entire ruin sway as though it were about to collapse.

The scene rapidly shattered and dimmed. Regardless of how the unimaginably large sea serpent struggled, it was unable to turn the situation around. All it could do was let out a long, painful scream in agony as it was reduced to points of light before disappearing into the gray fog.

Klein sat in The Fool's high-backed chair and quietly watched everything. For a long time, he made no unnecessary movements.

The gray fog silently spread out, and the scene seemed to return to its eternally unchanging state.

After nearly a minute, Klein leaned back in his chair, sighed, and said the serpent's name, "Sea God Kalvetua..."

He had sensed the abnormality upon coming into contact with the bone sword. During his conversation with Baldy Kalat, he had keenly detected that a faint yet extremely cold and sinister energy had somehow invaded his body and was slowly corrupting his soul.

Klein made a prompt decision and turned around to leave. He then sensed that there was a connection between the sinister power and his surroundings. It gradually strengthened and slowly connected to an unknown place.

Thus, while devoting part of his attention to resisting the corruption, he also controlled himself and avoided interacting with his surroundings.

Klein believed that if he responded to any abnormality on the journey back, it would only intensify the corruption, possibly to an irreversible extent.

At first, he had wanted to find a nearby washroom and use the gray fog's shielding and isolation to remove the cold, sinister power. After careful consideration, however, he decided to leave the area because so many Sea God followers were nearby that an accident was extremely likely.

During this process, Klein realized something else: if he dispelled it prematurely, the power projected by Sea God Kalvetua would leave remnants in his flesh and blood, with unknown consequences.

He had no choice but to wait patiently for an opportunity to "purify" himself, allowing the cold, sinister energy to permeate his spirit completely.

Reflecting on everything that had happened, Klein lightly rapped the edge of the table and muttered to himself, "It's not that strong..."

The Sea God's level was much lower than he had imagined!

His original plan had been to use the method that finished off Amon's avatar against Sea God Kalvetua's projection, which was trying to take over his body. However, before he could fuse with the Black Emperor card or throw out his paper angel, the gray fog directly dispelled Kalvetua without leaving a trace.

On this basis, Klein concluded that Sea God Kalvetua's level was inferior to Blasphemer Amon's. Although the latter might have been more adept at intrusion, it had only been an avatar.

Was it cast down from its pedestal by the Lord of Storms, or was it never even at the level of an angel? Is it merely a somewhat stronger demigod capable of responding to its believers' prayers within a certain range? Klein recalled what he had seen and realized that Sea God Kalvetua was in an abnormal state.

This evil spirit's very existence is unstable, as though it could collapse at any moment...

Moreover, it seemed to have fused with the spirit world within the ruins. Was that precisely how it escaped the Church of Storms' encirclement?

Klein leaned back in his chair and made a guess.

What happened today is definitely no coincidence. It stems from Archaeologist Leticia taking an important artifact from the forgotten temple. Sea God Kalvetua was already barely surviving, and that caused its condition to deteriorate so sharply that it could no longer maintain its existence...

While having its believers track the item, it also prepared to possess another body in order to survive. That bone sword must have come from its demigod body and concealed a trace of its projected power. Given a suitable target, the power would infiltrate the body of whoever touched it, corrupt their soul, and establish coordinates. This would allow its spirit to transfer there before it dissipated...

But clearly, it isn't adept at such matters. Yes, it isn't the Snake of Mercury. It can't create a closed loop of fate for its own reincarnation. Nor is it like Blasphemer Amon, who can parasitize a person's soul. If it truly possesses my body, its own body will immediately crumble and turn mine into a terrifying monster.

According to this logical development, the dying Kalvetua will likely engage in many crazy actions in the near future... Klein frowned. He didn't hesitate as he plummeted into the fog and returned to the real world.

He unlocked the door, opened it, and entered the living room, startling the pacing Danitz.

Danitz looked him up and down a few times and asked cautiously and warily, "Are... you alright?"

Klein maintained Gehrman Sparrow's persona and calmly replied, "It's settled."

Settled? Danitz looked around before glancing at the bedroom, wondering whether he had hallucinated the whole thing and there had never been a Sea God curse.

What did he do in the bedroom? He freed himself from the Sea God's curse in only a minute or two? Man, this guy has an enormous secret... Danitz took two steps back and made way.

Beside a table covered with a significant number of miscellaneous items, the wheelchair-bound baldy, Kalat, wore a look of regret as he said to the tattooed man, "What a pity."

"Just a little more... He didn't pick it up, just touched it." Edmonton sighed as well.

Kalat looked at the slightly bent bone sword and fervently said to himself, "When an outsider picks up that holy sword, God will walk the land again..."

Edmonton fell to his knees as well, as though confessing to a deity.

As time passed, both Kalat and Edmonton suddenly heard two miserable shrieks.

They looked up and saw that two of their companions had collapsed. Their skin was like a weathered rock, having lost all the moisture in their bodies.

Kalat and Edmonton glanced at each other and felt a peculiar atmosphere.

Both of them stood up at the same time and looked at the table.

That milky-white holy sword cracked loudly, splintering into countless small pieces.

Chapter 539: Late-night Operations

"When an outsider picks up that holy sword, God will walk the land again.

"But what happens if the holy sword shatters?"

These two sentences echoed in Kalat and Edmonton's minds; the matter lay entirely beyond their understanding.

For a few seconds, they stared at the shattered holy sword, their expressions dull while they remained speechless.

They couldn't believe that the holy sword, which had only been touched by an outsider in the evening, had suddenly shattered!

What does this mean? What does it represent? Neither of them dared think too deeply about it. They felt as though they had returned to the very beginning. Back then, the Loen military had discovered one of the Resistance's secret bases. It was raided without warning, and their fathers were killed in the massacre. Their female relatives were abducted and sold to different places. Before receiving the grievous news, Kalat and Edmonton had felt much as they did now: weighed down, confused, expectant, and uneasy, all those emotions combining into an intense pressure.

"Return to the forest, find the High Priest, and determine the reason. Perhaps this is God's latest revelation..." Kalat turned his wheelchair and said in a deep voice.

Edmonton immediately stood up, saying to his remaining subordinates, "Continue searching for those blasphemers, but do not stay here.

"Also, instruct the believers outside not to hold any rituals or even pray!"

The unexpected turn of events made him especially vigilant.

Bayam, at a street corner near the Cathedral of Waves.

Holding a stack of white folded paper, Danitz turned his head to the side, feeling a mix of nervousness, worry, and puzzlement.

"You mean I'm to put them up at different places along the street, then finally post one on the main door of the Cathedral of Waves?"

He was terrified that the cathedral doors would suddenly open and a group of priests and bishops brandishing their fists would rush out and attack them without even asking why he had put up the poster.

Klein maintained his coldness and said, "Yes."

His original plan had been to hand the matter of Sea God Kalvetua over to Mr. Hanged Man so that he could warn the Church of Storms. However, considering that Mr. Hanged Man still held the secret of Bansy Harbor and might already have reported it, providing another important piece of information so soon would easily arouse suspicion.

As for the solution, it was very simple. He could post a few posters on the Mandated Punishers' front door so that they could see it as soon as they came out.

There was a small problem with Klein's plan: he couldn't tell which shop around the Cathedral of Waves served as the Mandated Punishers' cover. All he could do was make Danitz work a little harder and paste the posters in prominent places everywhere, including, but not limited to, the cathedral's front door.

I should've run away earlier... Why did I think about the fact that this fellow had saved my life? Perhaps I would've been converted into gold pounds if he were any other powerful adventurer... No, who would've imagined that this lunatic would resolve the Sea God's curse so easily? Fleeing might have put me in an even worse situation... Lamenting inwardly, Danitz unfolded the stack of white paper and casually glanced at the contents.

"After Leticia Dolera and her companions entered and left the Sea God's ruins on Symeem Island, the Resistance pursued them. Meanwhile, Kalat and the Resistance were trying to sell a strange, slightly curved bone sword. Sea God Kalvetua's body is on the brink of collapse, and its mental state is extremely deranged."

"..."

Danitz froze for two seconds, then he subconsciously glanced at Gehrman Sparrow.

I can understand the first few statements, but why does it say that Sea God Kalvetua is on the brink of collapse and in an extremely deranged mental state... How does Gehrman Sparrow know? Did he discover something while dealing with the Sea God's curse? And how did he deal with that curse? The organization behind him is more powerful than I thought... Could it resemble the Aurora Order and serve a true deity? The more he thought about it, the more Danitz trembled in fear.

He had first heard of the Aurora Order during a robbery at sea. Back then, he had seen a serious expression on his captain's face for the first time. Afterward, she had taught him some so-called general knowledge of the mysterious world.

Klein responded to Danitz with an emotionless gaze.

In the poster, he had eliminated all subjective assumptions and described only matters that could be confirmed with certainty, so as not to interfere with the judgment of the Church of Storms' senior officials.

He had excluded the claim that Leticia and her companions had taken an important item. He replaced the lost temple with the broader term "Sea God's ruins." Kalat and the others had obviously abandoned the base after realizing that something had happened to the Sea God, so he left that unmentioned as well.

Danitz suddenly retracted his gaze, afraid to look any further.

Captain said that the more secrets someone has, the more dangerous they are! He thought for a while and worriedly said, "If we stick it on the front door of the cathedral, it will definitely garner immense attention from the Church of Storms.

"Will they discover that I was the one who did it?"

Klein gave a terse answer.

"Yes."

"..."

Danitz forced a smile and said, "Then wouldn't I be in grave danger?"

Klein used his Clown ability to control his expression and calmly replied, "You were already a pirate who has a bounty for your head."

Do you think you can swagger down the main streets of Bayam now? Klein lampooned inwardly.

That's true. No matter what, they will still capture me and claim my bounty... No, there's something wrong with that! Danitz blurted out, "But my bounty will increase!"

Klein looked at him without saying anything. He merely grinned.

For a moment, Danitz thought he heard a rhetorical question.

Isn't this a good thing?

A good thing, my ass! With a dry chuckle, Danitz took the stack of posters and, taking advantage of the strong winds and late hour, pasted them in prominent spots along the streets near the Cathedral of Waves.

It really does look like putting up small advertisements... Klein observed from afar with one hand in his pocket and commented inwardly.

He sighed and thought, It's good to have an assistant. At least I don't have to do something that tarnishes my reputation... If I were in Tingen or Backlund and something like that happened... The scene would be too beautiful to imagine...

Danitz finally reached outside the Cathedral of Waves, plastered the poster on the front door, clenched his fist, and pounded on the door.

After finishing all of this, he turned around and ran, as if there were ten Mandated Punishers running after him from behind.

Klein didn't dare be careless. He pulled out a paper figurine, snapped it open, and burned it to ashes before walking briskly toward another street.

After his recent encounters with the Mandated Punishers, he had gained a deep understanding of their style and didn't dare be the least bit careless.

It was only when they were far away from the Cathedral of Waves that they slowed down and regained their normal walking speed.

Danitz had a good constitution; his face wasn't flushed nor was he panting.

He was slightly puzzled and asked, "Why didn't you just write to the police or throw it in the governor-general's office?"

Before Klein could reply, he had already realized why.

That's right. The lower-ranking police and the staff of the governor-general's office are locals. They might well sympathize with the Resistance or secretly believe in the Sea God.

As they spoke, they turned a corner and saw an enormous red building ahead. The interior was brightly lit, and music could be heard from within. People and carriages streamed in and out through the door, giving no indication that the hour was late.

"Ha, we actually ended up walking here." After a second of hesitation, a smile appeared on Danitz's face, a smile that all men understood.

Red Theater? Klein, who was rich in theoretical knowledge, suddenly came to a realization.

Danitz laughed mischievously.

"This is one of the most famous places in the entire Sonia Sea. There are mysterious and flirtatious Balam girls, passionate Feynapotter girls, open and alluring Intis maidens, tall and graceful Feysac ladies, conservative and quiet Loen women, gentle and docile natives..."

This fellow knows a lot... He comes often? Klein swept a glance at Blazing and said nothing.

For some reason, Danitz felt as though he had been seen through, and he immediately laughed awkwardly.

"This is the sort of thing pirates boast about. I've only been here a few times.

"I didn't have much money in the past. I could only afford some ordinary ones, and that was mainly around the Fog Sea. Afterward, I joined the Golden Dream..."

No wonder... Although Vice Admiral Iceberg's men receive rather good benefits and often share in the treasure, it's still quite difficult to save enough to buy several houses in Bayam... Compared to the typical pirate, this fellow at least knows restraint and saves his money... Klein thought with sudden understanding.

Danitz didn't wish to continue the discussion, so he changed the subject.

"There are many streetwalkers in Bayam, especially over there."

He pointed into the distance and said, "A pirate once conducted an experiment. He knocked on random doors, offered money, and asked to sleep with the mistress of the house. In the end, three or four families out of ten agreed. Tsk, if it were someone like you, with the typical Loen look, almost no one would refuse. They might even hide their daughters to keep you from noticing them. Heh heh, Loen's Navy commits many murders and rapes here every year. They aren't any better than pirates, but they're merely sent back home and made to pay small fines."

Klein listened quietly, and he suddenly thought back to the evening when the believers of the Sea God were praying around the pool. He thought back to their feverish and numb faces.

Backlund, within the Odora family's villa.

Emlyn White, who had taken the initiative to expose certain problems about himself, nervously followed Cosmi underground. He once again arrived in the gray stone hall where the black iron coffin was kept.

"Esteemed Lord Nibbs, why have you summoned me?" Although Emlyn had rehearsed this scene in his mind dozens of times, he still couldn't completely remove the tension and apprehension in his heart.

In this state, he suddenly realized something. From the perspective of theater studies, the role he was playing should conceal his worries and fears.

There's no need to conceal anything deliberately... I did well! Emlyn suddenly became much calmer.

A deep, aged voice sounded from the coffin that was covered with symbols and magic labels.

"To reward you.

"For the Ancestor, you risked your life to pray to The Fool. Although you didn't receive a response, you bore an enormous risk. This is an act that needs to be rewarded.

"Here is an acceptance draft for 7,000 pounds as your reward. I couldn't give it to you promptly because of the Great Smog of Backlund, but it's never too late.

"At the same time, you must always be aware of yourself and not relax. If anything abnormal happens, immediately inform Cosmi."

He really gave me money... Emlyn almost forgot to close his mouth.

Chapter 540: Suppression

Even when Emlyn White left the Odora family's underground region, he still found things surreal. He couldn't believe that he could so easily be rewarded with 7,000 pounds.

The acceptance draft felt almost weightless in his hand, leaving him keenly aware of the absurdity of reality.

The Hanged Man's method was really effective... Instead of exposing and punishing me, Lord Nibbs gave me a generous reward. He did so solely to feign ignorance while encouraging me to make further attempts that he can observe in secret... Are the Sanguine's thoughts really that complicated too... Emlyn sighed, his mind adrift.

Quickly, he stopped his thoughts from wandering and turned his attention back to the reward of 7,000 pounds.

With this, I can buy that baron's remains from The Hanged Man and concoct it into a potion...

I'll soon become a baron--Sanguine Baron, Lord Emlyn White!

Emlyn's eyes lit up as his footsteps turned significantly brisk.

Among the Sanguine, anyone who wasn't granted the corresponding ritual by the elders would remain stuck at the level into which they were born. Advancing through the mere passage of time was almost impossible.

Unless one was extremely fortunate and had an elder relative nearing the end of their lifespan who had no choice but to bestow their powers upon a descendant through a ritual, most of Emlyn's Sanguine peers would remain stuck at "adulthood." They might have no chance of obtaining an "aristocratic title" for decades.

Emlyn's father and mother had lived for many years, but to this day, neither had become a baron. They couldn't even see any hope of doing so!

As Emlyn walked out the door, he couldn't help but glance at the nearby Cosmi Odora.

This elderly Sanguine, who was already active during the Roselle era, has only now become a baron... And I'm about to attain the same rank! One day, I will become a Sanguine marquis like Lord Nibbs! No, a duke, or even a prince! Only then will I be able to shoulder the responsibility of being the Sanguine's messiah... Yes, the baron's remains cost only about four thousand pounds. I'll have plenty of money left. I can even buy a few more dolls and give them new clothes... Emlyn unconsciously straightened his back and walked with increasing pride.

In the Berg household in the City of Silver.

Derrick lit some candles and prepared for the sacrificial ritual.

The lightning frequency alternated several times before he finally found an opportunity.

He was certain that the Chief couldn't keep an eye on him at that moment, because he had left the City of Silver with the somewhat recovered little boy, Jack, and a group of explorers to search for the so-called route leading from the coast to the destroyed city.

It didn't take long for Derrick to set up everything and say Mr. Fool's honorific name in a reverent, low voice.

With practiced care and precision, he sacrificed the Spirit Eater's stomach pouch needed by Miss Magician and the Beyonder ingredients requested by Mr. Hanged Man to the ruler above the gray fog.

There were many cracks on the City of Silver's solid city walls, but they were filled to the brim with black hard soil. Strands of dense weeds grew on them, swaying in the wind like human hair.

All of a sudden, all of them stood up as if they wanted to capture something, but they turned limp without any strength left in them.

Early in the morning, at the Cathedral of Waves.

Alger Wilson, who had come to collect the bounty, heard an important piece of news from the diocese bishop, Chogo.

Leticia Dolera of the Moses Ascetic Order had disguised herself as an archaeologist and entered the depths of Symeem Island's jungle. Whatever she had done had grievously injured Sea God Kalvetua, who had been in hiding for years, and pushed it to the verge of collapse. As a result, it was frantically searching for a means of survival.

Rumor had it that this evil spirit, which called itself the "Sea God," had been discovered long ago by two powerful military cardinals. After they defeated it with a Sealed Artifact, it had barely managed to escape and hide... This situation had persisted for more than a hundred years. Without any fundamental change, why would it suddenly collapse and become unable to maintain its existence? Alger frowned slightly, wondering what had changed.

A situation deliberately created by the Moses Ascetic Order?

But why didn't they do so in the past hundred years?

To complement one of their other plans?

Between speculation and doubt, Alger suddenly became aware of an important factor.

The biggest and most fundamental difference between the last hundred years and now was that The World had come to Bayam!

Mr. Fool's Blessed had come to the Rorsted Archipelago!

Does his appearance in a place indicate that something momentous is brewing or about to happen? First, there was the Great Smog of Backlund, and then the abnormality in Bansy Harbor. Now, there's Sea God Kalvetua's dying struggle and the Moses Ascetic Order's unknown plot... No, to be precise, wherever a major problem is brewing or about to occur, Mr. Fool's Blessed will be there! They follow Mr. Fool's arrangements and investigate the activities of secret organizations, evil gods, and angels! As soon as Alger's train of thought shifted, he experienced a sudden flash of insight, feeling as though he had grasped the truth.

It's not that things happen wherever The World is, but that The World and the other Blessed appear whenever something is about to happen!

What benefit does this bring Mr. Fool? Is the plan solely to thwart the enemy's schemes, or does it help "Him" lift his seal and release more power? Alger suppressed his doubts and decided to seize the opportunity to report the earlier intelligence.

He clenched his right hand into a fist and pressed it against his left breast. He said with a look of hesitation, "Your Excellency, while pursuing the remnant forces of Steel, I heard a piece of intel."

"What is it?" Chogo had originally planned to send this bishop-ranked captain out into the sea to search for traces of the Resistance, but he was forcibly interrupted by him. For a moment, he felt irritated.

Alger pretended to recall and said, "Someone mentioned Bansy Harbor in a conversation. They said an abnormality had occurred there, but the descendants of the Medici family were well hidden and weren't discovered."

Bansy Harbor, abnormality... Chogo took a step forward and pressed, "What else did they say?"

"Nothing else, apart from mentioning a fallen bishop of the Church. Your Excellency, is the Medici family a dark family from the Fourth Epoch?" Alger asked deliberately.

Chogo's expression sank.

"That's not something you should know.

"I'll report it to His Eminence Kottman at once."

Kottman was a Church of Storms Cardinal, the Archbishop of Rorsted Sea, a high-ranking deacon of the Mandated Punishers, and the true ruler of the Beyonder world in the archipelago, Jahn Kottman.

Chogo thought for a moment before asking, "Who was the person who mentioned this? What does he look like?"

Alger was long prepared.

"I don't know them, and they never showed up again.

"He seemed to notice that I was eavesdropping but he didn't show any anger. Instead, he smiled at me."

Alger had been describing Blasphemer Amon!

He wanted the cardinals to believe that the exposure of the secret regarding the Medici family and Bansy Harbor was the result of a struggle between the Kings of Angels.

He's truly the perfect person to use as an excuse... "He" wouldn't appear to defend himself, and even if "He" did, no one would believe it... And clearly, any divination concerning him would yield no result... If they use Beyonder means to verify my source, there will be strong signs of interference from Mr. Fool, preventing them from obtaining an accurate answer. From the looks of it, this is equivalent to something Amon might do... Alger thought with considerable relief.

Chogo nodded lightly.

"Find these people and trace the whereabouts of the Resistance."

"Yes, Your Excellency!" Alger answered with a look of piety and struck his right fist on his left breast.

Dark clouds covered the sky as the rain fell down in torrents, creating white fog in the process.

In the port of Bayam, the blue water rose slowly but firmly, and the freighters and liners rocked like leaves fluttering in midair.

The rain continued to fall, and the sea level gradually approached the dyke. Water flooded many parts of the city, and the atmosphere grew intensely oppressive.

One by one, the fanatical and numb locals, in their Taraba shirts and tattered jackets, came to the edge of the cliff or the dyke before jumping into the sea.

Their flesh rapidly lost moisture as they fell, and they were already desiccated corpses by the time they struck the sea.

A rioting group seized a Navy sailor as he walked out of a base. They furiously bit him to death, reducing him to a mangled, bloody mess and causing chaos in the streets.

Whoosh!

Boom!

The dyke collapsed, and the rising seawater poured into Bayam City.

When Klein jolted awake, his mind was filled with the scene from his dream.

For a Seer, this was a very clear revelation!

Sea God Kalvetua has failed to find a way to survive and has completely given up, causing it to go utterly mad. Does it intend to create a storm, stir up a tsunami, and drown the Rorsted Archipelago so that countless people can join it in death? At the same time, its devotees are beginning to commit extreme acts... I've already informed the Church of Storms. They'll likely be able to stop it... No, even if I hadn't informed them, they would definitely sense a problem when such a situation arose. They would then make the necessary response... Klein rolled to his feet, put on his clothes, and walked out of the bedroom.

He saw Blazing Danitz standing by the window, looking out.

He saw clouds hanging low as rain poured down in a seemingly endless torrent.

The signs have appeared? Klein walked over to Danitz, who was also gazing out at the oppressive weather. He could faintly hear the abnormal sound of the tide.

In the midst of silence, the sound of explosive thunder suddenly rang out in the air. The leaden-colored clouds quickly split apart, and the falling rain lost its source. In the direction of the docks, everything returned to normal.

The morning sun shone from high in the sky, bathing Bayam in holy light.

Danitz hissed and said to himself, "Jahn Kottman changed the weather..."

Jahn Kottman... The Church of Storms Cardinal and high-ranking deacon of the Mandated Punishers? He suppressed Kalvetua's powers? Klein withdrew his gaze in thought.

Danitz heaved a sigh of relief.

"I was rather worried just now. Heh heh, I was afraid that the Sea God, which is on the brink of collapse, would create a disaster.

"But there's Jahn Kottman."

"Captain said he's a Sequence 3 demigod. The potion's name is...

"It's... Sea King!"

Chapter 541: Visitor

Sea King... Upon hearing the name of Jahn Kottman's Sequence, the first thing Klein thought of was the complete honorific name of the Lord of Storms: "The King of the Skies, the Emperor of the Seas, the Lord of Calamity, the God of Storms!"

Sequence 3 is Sea King... Sequence 0 contains the elements of Sea Emperor, so it does match well... I wonder what Sequence 0 of the Storm pathway is called... Klein looked out the window again and saw that, although the weather was still gloomy, the morning sun was up. The sound of the tide had retreated, and the rain had stopped.

He reined in his thoughts and analyzed the situation using the information Danitz had revealed.

The potion is named Sea King, which means that the corresponding Sequence 3 demigods, Saints, have to act as Sea Kings. All acting rests on one premise: possessing all the necessary powers...

As a Sea King, he would definitely be a powerful ruler of the seas within his domain, even if that domain is limited. He would be able to walk freely on the seabed, summon tsunamis at will, raise the sea level, and control marine life... Fighting on their "home turf," Sea Kings are absolutely capable of suppressing all demigods of the same level... The King of the Five Seas and Queen Mystic are roughly at this level as well. Are the differences in strength merely due to their differing degrees of digestion and mastery of their powers?

But no matter what, as long as they're at sea, Sea Kings are nearly unbeatable...

With the abilities displayed by the Sea King, coupled with the oceanic environment, if one hasn't seen the Eternal Blazing Sun, True Creator, and other true gods, it isn't hard to understand why people would worship him, believe in him, and pray to him...

Many of the deities who were worshiped by primitive peoples might be at this level. They might not even be angels...

Yes, "do not look directly at God" refers to a true deity. Previously, Sea God Kalvetua didn't give me such a feeling, nor did Amon's avatar. Of course, I can't be sure if the true body of a King of Angels has some kind of attribute that also doesn't allow one to look directly at them...

One is a Sea God on the verge of collapse and, at most, a Sequence 3, while the other is a Sea King in good condition who can use Sealed Artifacts at any time. The outcome is obvious. Jahn Kottman would definitely be able to suppress Kalvetua's madness and prevent the sea from drowning Bayam. He would keep the entirety of Blue Mountain Island and its many cities from becoming ruins beneath the sea...

Just wait a few more days. Kalvetua's madness will dissipate completely, resolving the matter. This is the simplest and most effective way to deal with the situation, and it wouldn't bring about any panic at all. Most people here wouldn't even detect anything amiss.

However, there are also some problems. First, nothing else must go wrong. It's a mystery what that archaeologist, Leticia, did on Symeem Island. She had single-handedly brought about the Sea God's death. Perhaps the forces backing her--either the Moses Ascetic Order or Element Dawn--might seize the opportunity to seek something. This is the greatest latent danger. Yes, there should also be a High-Sequence Beyonder in the governor-general's office and the military. This is one of the kingdom's colonial bases in the Central Sonia Sea... Things shouldn't turn out too badly...

Then, there are the fanatical believers who will most likely become sacrifices as Kalvetua becomes madder and closer to its death. They are like stalks during the harvest, falling down in bunches.

However, for the Church of Storms, this isn't such a bad development. The most pious heretics would die with their deity, saving the Church the trouble of identifying and dealing with them. Those who aren't too pious wouldn't be affected much and could potentially be reformed... The only drawback is that the Rorsted Archipelago would suffer a labor shortage for a long time. Although that would be troublesome, it could be resolved if they gritted their teeth and endured it. Thus, the Church of Storms and Sea King Jahn Kottman are more likely to sit back and do nothing... Phew, I wonder how many people will die in the native enclaves and slums because of this... Klein's thoughts were suddenly interrupted by the synchronized tread of boots and a roar high in the sky.

Instinctively, he looked up and saw airships painted in dark-blue, with several cannon muzzles and machine guns sticking out, fly past him as they headed in different directions.

On the main street, soldiers in red coats, white pants, and black leather boots were lined up in neat rows. They were carrying rifles and dragging cannons as contingents of them moved past.

A tense, solemn atmosphere swept through the city.

This is a colony... A complicated mixture of emotions rose inexplicably within Klein, and he sighed to himself.

"I wonder how long it will take the Church to get rid of that sea serpent and find the place where it's hidden. That place is definitely a treasure trove with countless precious items..." Danitz, whose main job was a treasure hunter, suddenly spoke, his voice tinged with anticipation and regret.

His attitude had changed extremely quickly. He had already reduced the Sea God to a sea serpent, using "it" as a pronoun.

The Church won't do anything other than keep the matter suppressed. They'll see Kalvetua perish just from waiting for a few days... Of course, members of the Church of Storms might not have that kind of patience, especially when I've heard that Jahn Kottman's main job is being a high-ranking deacon of the Mandated Punishers. It's just that with the archipelago isolated overseas, he also works part-time as a cardinal to unify his authority. Perhaps his style of doing things is more like that of a Mandated Punisher... The place where Kalvetua is hiding? That ruin is almost integrated into the spirit world. Finding it wouldn't be so easy; otherwise, it wouldn't have led such an abject existence until today... At this thought, Klein suddenly had an idea.

Finding the ruin where Kalvetua was hiding would be extremely difficult from the real world. Since it has already perished, it's not something that can be found in a year or so, unless more accurate information is obtained.

But if the search was done from the spirit world?

The spirit world would make it even harder to pinpoint its location, but there are still options. Snake of Mercury Will Auceptin had once done so with the help of his folded crane... I still have no idea how to do that exactly, but that's not important. I can ask Mr. Azik. Death's domain controls part of the authority of the spirit world... The Underworld, or should I say "Hell," was created by Phoenix Ancestor Gregrace in the spirit world... A thought flashed through Klein's mind. He took out the ancient and exquisite copper whistle, put it to his mouth, and blew into it.

He believed that even if he returned to the bedroom or entered the washroom, the messenger's massive body would be discovered by Danitz, who similarly had a nontrivial spiritual perception, so he didn't avoid him.

Danitz was imagining the treasures of Sea God Kalvetua when he suddenly felt a chill at his neck.

His spiritual intuition stirred, and he quickly activated his Spirit Vision before looking to the side. There, he saw white bones spewing out from the floor and flying upwards, forming a giant skeleton with an illusory head that pierced through the ceiling.

The skeleton lowered its head slightly; the two pitch-black flames in its eye sockets were visible even through the ceiling.

The oppressive presence of its massive body made Danitz jump to the side. His body was half-bent, and he conjured a scarlet flame in the palm of his right hand.

What kind of monster is this? Danitz looked at Gehrman Sparrow in surprise, only to see him holding a copper whistle and lifting his head as he looked at the skeletal monster.

Klein looked up at the huge messenger while it looked down at him. Both parties seemed frozen in place.

...Tsk, I was too anxious. I summoned the messenger before I even wrote the letter... Should I make it stay here or let it return first and let it come back later? I'm Gehrman Sparrow now, yes-- Gehrman Sparrow! Klein didn't say anything. He calmly withdrew his gaze, unhurriedly found pen and paper, and began to write the letter.

Klein folded the paper and turned to see that the messenger had clenched its hand at some point.

Its body suddenly disintegrated, and its bones crumbled down, drilling into the floor.

Messenger? Danitz was stunned for a moment before understanding what he meant.

Such a huge and terrifying monster is actually a messenger used for delivering letters? Indeed, there's a powerful secret organization behind Gehrman Sparrow! I-if I had such a messenger, everyone on the ship would envy me. It would be really, really cool! Danitz thought of how he would brag and flaunt his messenger when he returned to the ship.

Putting away Azik's copper whistle, Klein pulled over a chair and sat down, ready to eat breakfast shortly.

After a while, he heard knocking at the door.

With wrinkles at the corners of his eyes, Elland looked into the room and chuckled.

"When you were checking into this hotel, you used Gehrman Sparrow's name for the registration. It was easy to find with a quick search."

That's because this identity is quite proper and innocent. Besides, in the eyes of the Church of Storms, I belong to the military... Klein slowly stood up and said to Elland, "What happened?"

Elland pointed outside and said, "Something happened in Bayam, but it's not a big problem. The military has joined forces with the Church, and they're currently conducting a search and arrest operation throughout the city. Heh heh, they believe that although you're of unknown origin, you're very friendly to us. They hope you can help with the search and prevent any accidents from happening. This is all thanks to you for exaggerating my degree of friendliness... That is to say, after the Church of the Goddess and the Church of Steam and Machinery, I can once again receive money from the military? For a moment, Klein felt mixed feelings.

"Okay."

Chapter 542: Bayam Under Curfew

Seeing Gehrman Sparrow put on his coat and hat and pick up his cane, Danitz recalled that he had apparently been forgotten.

He coughed once, and under the gaze of two pairs of eyes, he said, "D-do I need to follow?"

It's best not to... Who knows what would happen! Before this, we merely stopped at Bansy Harbor, yet we ended up encountering an extremely bizarre situation. Last night, I brought Gehrman Sparrow to visit the Resistance's liaison and ended up embroiled in the Sea God's curse. If I follow this madman to search for Leticia and the other archaeologists today, who knows what will happen? Danitz lowered his gaze to his left arm, which was still in a splint. In a matter of days, he felt that he had experienced more than he normally would in months or even half a year.

Someone will sweep this area? And then the great pirate Blazing would be caught and turned into gold pounds? Danitz frowned and laughed dryly.

If I don't disguise myself, a great pirate like me taking part in an operation with the military and the Church will only result in my immediate arrest... Danitz imagined himself pinned down with a knee crushing his back, struggling like a catfish.

After thinking for a few seconds, Elland took out an iron-black mask from his inside pocket and tossed it over.

"Just put it on. I will explain the rest."

Yes, there's no need to waste time on useless disguises... Klein evaluated inwardly.

Without a word, he turned the handle and walked out of the room.

Elland followed close behind, and Danitz hurried to catch up with them as he grabbed his coat and put on the iron mask.

When they reached the street where there was deep floodwater but no pedestrians, Klein pressed down on his hat and asked, "How do we start?"

"In regions.

"My Beyonder profession has some special characteristics. As long as I see a person in the flesh, in a photograph, or in a sketch, I'll be able to commit the target's appearance firmly to memory and gain an additional supernatural sense. Sequence 8 Sheriff of the Arbiter pathway... Klein nodded thoughtfully and asked as they walked, "Do you have their belongings?"

In other words, Leticia and company haven't left by boat yet... Klein understood what Elland meant.

Blue Mountain Island was the largest island in the Rorsted Archipelago. It was vast, with dense forests and abundant mineral resources. Consequently, the island had many cities, all built around fertile land or astonishingly rich mineral deposits.

To seize this wealth, the Loen Kingdom first bribed the indigenous princes, then resorted to force, and finally set up the governor-general's office. In a more efficient manner, it opened wide roads leading to cities and completed several important railways--this was in the form of establishing a corresponding railway company to sell shares and raise funds on the Backlund Stock Exchange.

Of course, these large projects were accompanied by the deaths of many local people, dangerous construction sites, excessive work, almost slave-like treatment, and meager wages, which allowed bodies to be buried one after another under the roadbed and railroad ties.

To this day, a large number of locals still loathed the railway, believing that it had devoured countless lives and brought untold suffering. It was a symbol of evil gods and devils.

Elland turned his head to glance at Danitz and said, "If they leave by land, then there's nothing to worry about."

It's very simple. The roads leading through the forest are controlled by the Resistance, and the majority of the Resistance are believers of the Sea God. Hence, how would Leticia and the others, who were responsible for Kalvetua's breakdown, dare to pass through these regions at night? If they dared to, then it can only mean one thing; they didn't realize the severity of the consequences of what they had done in the Sea God ruins on Symeem Island. This also negates the conjecture that either the Moses Ascetic Order or Element Dawn has other motives... Klein controlled the urge to shake his head, and he followed Elland into another street.

I drew this woman... Klein glanced at it before tossing it to Danitz.

Surrounding teams would rush over to reinforce them. If they're ordinary pirates or criminals, we are to handle them ourselves. Let's wait. Perhaps, it's because the fireworks couldn't be released in time..."

While he was talking, the glass on the third story of the house facing the street shattered with a crack. A bear-like brawny man jumped down. His speed was extremely fast as he ran into the distance like a cheetah.

At this moment, an enormous shadow enveloped him, and Gatling sounds came from the sky above.

The brawny man's body was almost torn apart by the machine gun fire as he fell to the ground without putting up any resistance. Blood flowed out and dyed the ground red. If the residents weren't forbidden to leave their homes, then they would've screamed.

"Goltadt." Danitz recognized the victim.

Their clothes were tattered and their faces gaunt, clearly indicating that they were natives.

A little further away, several young native children were hiding in the corner. They were quietly looking at them in fear. Their eyes were dark and their faces were dirty.

Backlund, Cherwood Borough.

Fors lifted the ceramic cup from the table and felt the heat.

She roused herself and quietly waited for any changes.

The temperature of the hot water dropped rapidly, and a thin layer of ice appeared on the surface of the liquid. White frost appeared on the rim of the cup.

She didn't waste any time after receiving the Spirit Eater's stomach pouch, immediately concocting the potion and completing the advancement. She obtained quite a number of spells with lower potency.

Among them, Fors's favorites were Fog, Wind, Flash, Freezing, Electric Shock, and Tumble, which made people slip.

Only at this point did she feel that she was a complete Beyonder. She was no longer someone who could only pass through walls or rely solely on ritualistic magic.

At almost noon, Elland, with the help of Klein and Danitz, had completed most of the investigation.

Glancing at the "dark-blue monster" flying in his direction, Danitz let out a hollow laugh and quickened his pace.

Two minutes later, they arrived at their destination and saw a house with a lawn facing the street. Three or four military personnel were lying on the ground. Their faces were pale, and their bodies were trembling as if they had been thrown into a frozen lake.

The farther Klein walked in that direction, the colder it became. It was as if he had arrived in the polar regions.

Soon, he discovered that the ditches outside the house were filled with thick snow.

Just then, a burst of female laughter came from inside the house at varying pitches, alternately frenzied and eerie.

"Hahaha...

"Hahaha...

Chapter 543: The Reality That Exceeds Expectations

The target appears to have been found... Klein looked away and made a preliminary judgment.

As there were no portraits, the dead adventurer's face was burned black, which meant that Elland clearly didn't recognize this as one of their targets. After observing the situation and listening to the creepy laughter for a few seconds, he pointed to the three or four military personnel lying outside the house.

"Drag them back first, then wait for the other teams to arrive before launching our attack.

"Or..."

He hesitated for a moment, then he raised his head to look at the approaching dark-blue airship.

Without another word, and without instructing Klein and Danitz, Elland ran to the unconscious military personnel whose faces were purple.

Tap. Tap. Tap... The closer he got, the weaker his steps became. In the end, his body became stiff and every step he took became extremely difficult.

Elland, who had been the boatswain of the Imperial Navy, was experienced. He decisively stopped moving forward and turned around slowly, walking back one step at a time.

The farther he retreated, the easier his movements became. However, he was still shaking uncontrollably, and his eyebrows and temples were covered in a thin layer of frost.

Freezing this rapidly defies common sense... Extreme cold reminiscent of a disaster... Elland's attempt allowed Klein to grasp how dangerous the area was, and he could only sigh inwardly.

Unfortunately, the Sun Brooch isn't able to produce actual heat. It only stimulates the mind. Although it can produce tangible effects in the body, it will only allow a person to withstand this cold for three to four seconds at most...

Looking at Elland's chattering teeth and how he was unable to open his mouth despite wanting to say something, Klein's gaze swept over Danitz.

He threw his cane and said in a low voice, "Fire."

Fire? Danitz was stunned at first, but he quickly understood Gehrman Sparrow.

He had also witnessed the entire process of Elland's failure!

A scarlet fireball that couldn't be considered bright formed on Danitz's right palm, and he threw it beside the military personnel.

The fireball traveled for nearly twenty meters and then landed on the ground without exploding. Instead, it quietly rose into the air.

The scarlet pillar of fire emitted sizzling sounds as it continuously shrank and quickly dimmed.

Suddenly, it expanded, as though making one last desperate struggle before perishing.

Klein, wearing a black tweed coat, jumped out and landed right next to the military personnel.

He bent down, reached out with both hands, and grabbed the man's clothes.

Then, he planted his feet, drove power through his waist, and threw the man out.

The serviceman immediately flew through the air and landed ten meters away, safely outside the coldest part of the area.

After doing this, Klein snapped his fingers and lit a match in his pocket before the chill seeped into his body.

The scarlet streams of flames gushed out like water, instantly enveloping him.

By the time everything faded, Klein had disappeared from his location.

Flames leaped out, flickering and extinguishing from time to time. With the help of Danitz's fireballs and his own matches, Klein repeatedly phased about in the extremely cold area, easily throwing out several military personnel.

After two to three attempts, he carried the last member of the military back to his original position.

Elland had clearly recovered as he gave a thumbs up.

"I'm very glad and honored to have made the decision to ask for your help today."

Captain, I like your tactful praise... Also, remember to raise the remuneration... Klein nodded politely, half turned, and looked at the open windows of the house. He heard the laughter grow stranger.

Danitz pursed his lips to the side and silently cursed Elland.

Did you not see the contribution I made?

Although my fireball has become something akin to a prop for a magic show, it still made actual contributions!

This guy's nickname is Just Elland, but he's not just at all!

As he muttered, a shadow loomed over the area, and the airship had arrived overhead.

"Evacuate the people in the surrounding houses!" an officer shouted from the airship.

After Elland and two other teams cleared several nearby buildings, the airship lowered its altitude and adjusted its cannon muzzles.

Boom! Boom! Boom!

The cannons fired continuously, bombarding the house which was still filled with creepy laughter.

Hearing the explosions and seeing the flashes of flames, Klein sighed as he held his cane.

This was the artillery bombardment he had advocated. He had once proposed it in Tingen City but was unable to put it into practice. Today, however, the colonial forces were carrying it out before his eyes.

Elland and the others stood guard in different positions amid the deafening cannon fire, so as to prevent the people or perhaps monsters inside from breaking out.

Soon the house collapsed, and smoke rose from the piles of masonry. All the snow and ice was gone.

Suddenly, a thick bolt of lightning flashed and struck the airship head-on.

Klein frowned. He saw that the airship was completely still and heard a steam boiler produce a jarring sound.

The dark-blue monster in the sky lost some control. Thick smoke began to billow out as it began listing downward.

Seems like they're being shielded by ammunition and being protected by an outer layer of air... I thought it would cause an explosion, blasting the airship to pieces... Klein turned his full attention back to the collapsed house.

When he initially felt the extreme coldness and saw the male adventurer's charred body, he thought he had encountered another demoness--one who grasped the Witch potion formula. Having dealt with demonesses several times, he knew that Beyonders of this pathway could control ice and black flames from Sequence 7.

But that flash of lightning had forced him to discard that judgment. He believed that Leticia was indeed a member of the Moses Ascetic Order or Element Dawn, a real woman.

The moment Klein arrived at this conclusion, the pile of bricks and wood was flung aside. A bright red, charred figure crawled out, propping itself up on its elbows.

The figure was female, and Leticia's original appearance was barely recognizable. Elland and the others were shocked to discover that they had already found their target. However, compared to before, Leticia's current state was both terrifying and miserable.

Her body was covered in black spots. Artillery shells had torn bloody holes across her body. White fascia that seemed to have a life of its own was squirming inside her body.

The top of her head had split open, and her brain oozed out, sticking to the surface like the palms of children overlapping each other.

Her gray eyes were out of focus, one of them blazing with fire, the other flashing with lightning.

Two heads that were screaming in pain were embedded in her abdomen beneath her chest. They were the other two male adventurers.
